  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lorelei adalah ondina atau peri air Sungai Rhein dalam legenda Jerman, yang memikat para pelaut dengan kecantikan dan nyanyiannya dari batu karang yang menyandang namanya hingga kapal mereka karam.",
    "en": "Lorelei is an undine or water nymph of the Rhine in German legend, who lured boatmen with her beauty and song from the rock that bears her name until their vessels were wrecked.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lorelei-c01",
      "lorelei-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda, Lorelei adalah ondina cantik Sungai Rhein yang memikat para lelaki dengan nyanyian dan rupanya, sehingga terjadi kapal karam dan bencana. Batu karang tempat ia menurut tradisi menampakkan diri hingga kini masih menyandang namanya.",
      "en": "According to legend, Lorelei was a beautiful undine of the Rhine who drew men to her with her singing and looks, bringing shipwreck and disaster. The rock where tradition says she appeared still carries her name today.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lorelei-c01",
        "lorelei-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia konon semula gadis cantik yang dikhianati lelaki yang dicintainya lalu terjun dari tebing karena tak sanggup menanggung pedihnya; artikel bahasa Turki menyebutnya ingin membalas dendam kepada para pelaut setelah dikhianati seorang nelayan kekasihnya.",
      "en": "She is said to have first been a lovely girl betrayed by the man she loved, who threw herself from a cliff because she could not bear the pain; the Turkish article says she sought revenge on boatmen after being deceived by the fisherman she loved.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lorelei-c04",
        "lorelei-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Suatu hari, untuk membalas kematian putranya, seorang bangsawan mengirim prajurit yang tahan terhadap pesonanya untuk membunuh Lorelei. Mereka hampir berhasil, tetapi Lorelei memanggil ayahnya, yang mengirim seekor kuda buih untuk membawanya ke dasar sungai; ia tak pernah kembali, dan sejak itu para pelaut tidak lagi perlu takut padanya.",
      "en": "One day a nobleman, avenging his dead son, sent soldiers who could resist her charm to kill Lorelei. They nearly succeeded, but she called on her father, who sent a horse of foam to carry her down into the depths of the river; she never returned, and from then on boatmen no longer had to fear her.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lorelei-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "lorelei-c01",
      "source_id": "lorelei-s1",
      "quote": "Según la leyenda, fue una hermosa sirena del río Rin que atraía a los hombres desde la roca que lleva su nombre con su belleza y sus cantos, causando naufragios y desastres.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, Lorelei adalah siren cantik Sungai Rhein (Rin) yang memikat para lelaki dari batu karang bernama sama dengan kecantikan dan nyanyiannya, menyebabkan kapal karam dan bencana.",
        "en": "According to legend she was a beautiful siren of the Rhine (Rin) who drew men from the rock bearing her name with her beauty and songs, causing shipwrecks and disasters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lorelei-c02",
      "source_id": "lorelei-s2",
      "quote": "Lorelei è una figura leggendaria della Germania.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lorelei adalah tokoh legenda dari Jerman (Germania).",
        "en": "Lorelei is a legendary figure of Germany (Germania)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lorelei-c03",
      "source_id": "lorelei-s2",
      "quote": "Secondo la leggenda, Lorelei era una bellissima ondina del fiume Reno, che attirava a sé gli uomini col suo canto e con il suo aspetto, causando naufragi e sciagure.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, Lorelei adalah ondina cantik Sungai Rhein (Reno) yang memikat para lelaki dengan nyanyian dan rupanya, menyebabkan kapal karam dan bencana; batu karang tempat ia menampakkan diri masih menyandang namanya.",
        "en": "According to legend Lorelei was a lovely undine of the Rhine (Reno) who drew men with her song and looks, causing shipwrecks and misfortune; the rock where she appeared still bears her name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lorelei-c04",
      "source_id": "lorelei-s1",
      "quote": "Según se creía, originariamente era una hermosa muchacha que fue traicionada por el hombre al que amaba, tras lo cual se arrojó de un acantilado por no poder soportar el dolor.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon ia semula gadis cantik yang dikhianati lelaki yang dicintainya, lalu terjun dari tebing karena tak sanggup menanggung pedihnya.",
        "en": "She was believed to have first been a beautiful girl betrayed by the man she loved, who then threw herself from a cliff, unable to bear the pain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lorelei-c05",
      "source_id": "lorelei-s3",
      "quote": "Efsaneye göre âşık olduğu balıkçının kendisini aldatması üzerine gemicilerden öç almak istemektedir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Turki, legenda menyebut ia ingin membalas dendam kepada para pelaut setelah nelayan kekasihnya mengkhianatinya.",
        "en": "Per the Turkish article, the legend says she wants revenge on sailors after the fisherman she loved deceived her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lorelei-c06",
      "source_id": "lorelei-s2",
      "quote": "Un giorno, per vendicare il figlio morto, un nobile inviò i suoi soldati a uccidere la fanciulla, soldati che seppero resistere al suo incanto e che l'avrebbero uccisa, se lei non avesse chiamato suo padre in aiuto: egli mandò un cavallo di schiuma che condusse la ninfa nelle profondità del fiume, da cui ella non fece più ritorno.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk membalas kematian putranya, seorang bangsawan mengirim prajurit yang tahan pesona Lorelei untuk membunuhnya; ayahnya mengirim kuda buih yang membawanya ke dasar sungai dan ia tak pernah kembali.",
        "en": "To avenge his dead son, a nobleman sent soldiers immune to her charm to kill her; her father sent a horse of foam that took the nymph to the river depths, from which she never returned."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lorelei-c07",
      "source_id": "lorelei-s2",
      "quote": "Questa figura leggendaria si ritrova spesso nei componimenti dei poeti tedeschi dell'epoca romantica, come ad esempio in Das Lied der Loreley (1827) di Heinrich Heine o in Waldgespräch (1815) di Joseph Freiherr von Eichendorff. Fu inoltre proprio da questo mito che il compositore Alfredo Catalani trasse ispirazione per la sua opera Loreley.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tokoh ini sering muncul dalam karya penyair Jerman era Romantik, seperti Das Lied der Loreley (1827) karya Heinrich Heine dan Waldgespräch (1815) karya Joseph Freiherr von Eichendorff; komponis Alfredo Catalani terinspirasi mitos ini untuk operanya Loreley.",
        "en": "The figure often appears in German Romantic poetry, as in Heinrich Heine's Das Lied der Loreley (1827) or Joseph Freiherr von Eichendorff's Waldgespräch (1815); composer Alfredo Catalani drew on the myth for his opera Loreley."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Orang lumut (Moosleute) adalah golongan peri dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, bertubuh kecil, berbalut lumut, dan hidupnya terikat erat pada pohon dan hutan.",
    "en": "Moss people (Moosleute) are a class of fairy-folk in German folklore, small in size, clad in moss and bound closely to trees and the forest.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "moss-people-c01",
      "moss-people-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Orang lumut, juga disebut orang kayu (Holzleute), orang hutan (Waldleute), atau orang liar, adalah golongan peri dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang dibandingkan dengan kurcaci, elf, atau roh. Kata Schrat dan Waldschrat juga dipakai untuk mereka. Moosweiblein, yang perempuan, dikenal di Sachsen, Thüringen, Harz, Vogtland, Oberpfalz, Bayerischen Wald, Franken, Riesengebirge, hingga Westfalen.",
      "en": "Moss people, also called wood folk (Holzleute), forest folk (Waldleute) or wild folk, are a class of fairy-folk in German folklore compared with dwarfs, elves or spirits. The words Schrat and Waldschrat are used for them too. The female Moosweiblein are known in Saxony, Thuringia, the Harz, the Vogtland, the Upper Palatinate, the Bavarian Forest, Franconia, the Giant Mountains and as far as Westphalia.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "moss-people-c01",
        "moss-people-c02",
        "moss-people-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka biasanya sebesar anak-anak, kelabu dan tampak tua, berbulu, dan berpakaian lumut, meski kadang lebih besar atau malah cantik. Hidup mereka terikat pada pohon: bila kulit dalam sebuah pohon terkelupas karena gesekan, seorang perempuan hutan mati. Mereka tinggal di rongga tanah, pohon berlubang, atau pondok lumut, tidur di atas lumut, dan bisa menikah serta beranak.",
      "en": "They are usually the size of children, grey and old-looking, hairy and dressed in moss, though sometimes larger or even pretty. Their lives are bound to trees: if a tree's inner bark is rubbed loose, a wood-woman dies. They live in earth holes, hollow trees or moss huts, sleep on moss, and can marry and have children.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "moss-people-c03",
        "moss-people-c04",
        "moss-people-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka kadang meminjam barang atau meminta bantuan manusia dan selalu membalas dengan murah hati, sering dengan nasihat atau roti, tetapi mudah tersinggung bila hadiahnya ditolak atau bila diberi roti jintan, yang mereka benci. Sebagian mitos menyebut mereka meminta ASI untuk bayi mereka atau mencuri anak manusia. Perempuan lumut dapat menyebarkan wabah, tetapi juga menunjukkan tanaman obat untuk menyembuhkannya.",
      "en": "They sometimes borrow things or ask people for help and always repay generously, often with advice or bread, but are easily offended if their gifts are spurned or if given caraway bread, which they hate. Some myths have them asking for breast milk for their young or stealing human children. Moss women can spread plague, yet also show people the healing herbs against it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "moss-people-c05",
        "moss-people-c06",
        "moss-people-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka sering menjadi buruan perburuan liar. Untuk menyelamatkan diri, mereka berlindung di tunggul pohon yang ditandai tiga salib oleh penebang saat pohon itu tumbang.",
      "en": "They are often the quarry of the Wild Hunt. To escape, they take refuge on tree stumps that a woodsman marked with three crosses while the tree was falling.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "moss-people-c10"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "en": "Moss people were said to hate caraway bread and were often heard crying 'Kümmelbrot, unser Tod!' ('Caraway bread, our death!').",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c01",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "are a class of fairy-folk, variously compared to dwarfs, elves, or spirits, described in German folklore as having an intimate connection to trees and the forest.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang lumut (Moosleute), juga orang kayu (Holzleute) atau orang hutan (Waldleute), adalah golongan peri yang dibandingkan dengan kurcaci, elf, atau roh, digambarkan dalam cerita rakyat Jerman (German) memiliki ikatan erat dengan pohon dan hutan.",
        "en": "Moss people (Moosleute), also wood folk (Holzleute) or forest folk (Waldleute), are a class of fairy-folk compared with dwarfs, elves or spirits, described in German folklore as intimately tied to trees and the forest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c02",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "In German, the words Schrat and Waldschrat are also used for a moss person.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Jerman, kata Schrat dan Waldschrat juga dipakai untuk orang lumut.",
        "en": "In German, Schrat and Waldschrat also denote a moss person."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c03",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "The moss people are sometimes described as similar to dwarfs, being the same size as children, but \"grey and old-looking, hairy, and clad in moss.\" Sometimes, moss folk are also bigger. In other descriptions they are said to be pretty.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang lumut kadang digambarkan mirip kurcaci, sebesar anak-anak, tetapi kelabu, tampak tua, berbulu, dan berbalut lumut; kadang lebih besar, dan dalam deskripsi lain cantik.",
        "en": "Moss people are sometimes like dwarfs, child-sized but grey, old-looking, hairy and moss-clad; sometimes bigger, and in other accounts pretty."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c04",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "They are similar to hamadryads. Their lives are \"attached to the trees; if any one causes by friction the inner bark to loosen a Wood-woman dies.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka mirip hamadryad: hidup mereka terikat pada pohon, dan bila seseorang membuat kulit dalam pohon terkelupas karena gesekan, seorang perempuan hutan mati.",
        "en": "They resemble hamadryads: their lives are bound to the trees, and if someone rubs loose a tree's inner bark, a wood-woman dies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c05",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "According to legend, these fairies would occasionally borrow items from people or ask for help but would always compensate the owners generously, often with either good advice or bread. It was, however, easy to anger such wood-sprites, either by spurning their gifts (which might be the compensations named above) or by giving them caraway bread",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, peri-peri ini kadang meminjam barang atau minta bantuan tetapi selalu membalas dengan murah hati, sering dengan nasihat atau roti; mereka mudah marah bila hadiahnya ditolak atau diberi roti jintan, sambil berseru \"Kümmelbrot, unser Tod!\".",
        "en": "Legend says these fairies sometimes borrowed things or asked for help but always repaid generously, often with advice or bread; they were easily angered if their gifts were spurned or if given caraway bread, crying 'Kümmelbrot, unser Tod!'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c06",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "In certain myths, the moss folk would ask humans for breast milk to feed their young, or steal little human children – motifs found in changeling lore.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian mitos, orang lumut meminta ASI kepada manusia untuk bayi mereka atau mencuri anak manusia, motif yang ada dalam cerita anak tukar (changeling).",
        "en": "In some myths the moss folk ask people for breast milk for their young or steal human children, motifs of changeling lore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c07",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "Moss people, especially the females of the species, are able to send plagues on one hand; on the other, they can also heal the victims of such plagues. During epidemics the Holzfräulein (\"Wood ladies\") would emerge from the forest to show the people which medicinal herbs could cure or ward off plague.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang lumut, terutama yang perempuan, dapat mendatangkan wabah tetapi juga menyembuhkan korbannya; saat epidemi Holzfräulein keluar dari hutan untuk menunjukkan tanaman obat penyembuh atau penangkal wabah.",
        "en": "Moss people, especially females, can send plagues yet also heal their victims; in epidemics the Holzfräulein came out of the forest to show which herbs cure or prevent plague."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c08",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s2",
      "quote": "Moosweiblein treten in Sachsen, Thüringen, an der Saale, im Orlagau, im Harz, im Vogtland, in der Oberpfalz, im Bayerischen Wald, in Franken, in Oberfranken, im Böhmerwald, um Warnsdorf in Nordtschechien, im Riesengebirge und in Westfalen auf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Moosweiblein muncul di Sachsen, Thüringen, di tepi Saale, Orlagau, Harz, Vogtland, Oberpfalz, Bayerischen Wald, Franken, Oberfranken, Böhmerwald, sekitar Warnsdorf di Nordtschechien, Riesengebirge, dan Westfalen.",
        "en": "Moosweiblein appear in Sachsen, Thüringen, an der Saale, Orlagau, Harz, Vogtland, Oberpfalz, Bayerischen Wald, Franken, Oberfranken, Böhmerwald, around Warnsdorf in Nordtschechien, Riesengebirge and Westfalen (Saxony, Thuringia, Upper Palatinate, Bavarian Forest, Franconia, Giant Mountains, Westphalia)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c09",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s2",
      "quote": "Die Moosweiblein leben im Wald. Sie wohnen auf der Heide, an dunklen Orten im Holz oder im tiefsten Wald. Ihre Behausung ist ein Erdloch, ein hohler Baum, ein Häuslein aus Baumwurzeln oder eine Mooshütte. Sie schlafen auf Moos und betten auch ihre Kinder auf Moos oder Rinde.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Moosweiblein tinggal di hutan, di padang, di tempat gelap, atau di hutan terdalam; rumahnya rongga tanah, pohon berlubang, pondok dari akar, atau pondok lumut; mereka tidur di atas lumut dan membaringkan anak-anaknya di atas lumut atau kulit kayu.",
        "en": "Moosweiblein live in the forest, on heaths, in dark places or the deepest woods; they dwell in earth holes, hollow trees, root huts or moss huts, sleep on moss and bed their children on moss or bark."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c10",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "In Thuringia, the only place where a Moosweibchen \"little moss woman\") is safe from the wild huntsman (German wilder Jäger) chasing her kind in the afternoon and at night is a tree stump marked with three crosses by a woodsman while the tree was still falling.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Thüringen (Thuringia), satu-satunya tempat aman bagi Moosweibchen dari pemburu liar (Wild Hunt; wilder Jäger) adalah tunggul pohon yang ditandai tiga salib oleh penebang saat pohon itu masih tumbang.",
        "en": "In Thuringia the only refuge for a Moosweibchen from the wild huntsman (the Wild Hunt; wilder Jäger) is a stump marked with three crosses by a woodsman while the tree was still falling."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moss-people-c11",
      "source_id": "moss-people-s1",
      "quote": "The female Moss people, the Moosfräulein (\"Moss ladies\"), have a queen called the Buschgroßmutter (Buschgrossmutter; \"Shrub Grandmother\").",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para perempuan lumut, Moosfräulein, memiliki ratu bernama Buschgroßmutter (\"Nenek Semak\").",
        "en": "The female moss people, the Moosfräulein, have a queen called the Buschgroßmutter ('Shrub Grandmother')."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Selkolla (\"kepala anjing laut\") adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Islandia, perempuan cantik yang kadang berkepala anjing laut, musuh Uskup Guðmundur Arason.",
    "en": "Selkolla ('seal-head') is a supernatural being of Icelandic folklore, a fair woman sometimes seen with a seal's head, remembered as the adversary of Bishop Guðmundur Arason.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Selkolla adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Islandia: perempuan cantik yang kadang terlihat berkepala anjing laut. Ia terutama dikenal sebagai lawan Uskup Guðmundur Arason (1161–1237), dan kisahnya terkenal karena memadukan gagasan Kristen dengan konsep tradisi Skandinavia pra-Kristen. Sumber abad pertengahan utamanya adalah versi B dan D Guðmundar saga Arasonar, dalam bagian yang kini disebut Selkollu þáttr; ia pertama kali disebut dalam Íslendinga saga karya Sturla Þórðarson. Kisah ini juga dianggap mengingatkan pada cerita rakyat yang lebih kemudian tentang anak-anak orang tersembunyi dan hantu anak.",
      "en": "Selkolla is a supernatural being of Icelandic folklore: a fair woman who is sometimes seen with a seal's head. She is known above all as an opponent of Bishop Guðmundur Arason (1161–1237), and her tales are noted for blending Christian ideas with concepts from pre-Christian Scandinavian tradition. Her main medieval source is versions B and D of Guðmundar saga Arasonar, in the episode now called Selkollu þáttr; she is first mentioned in Sturla Þórðarson's Íslendinga saga. The tale is also thought to recall later folk stories of hidden-folk children and child ghosts.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "selkolla-c01",
        "selkolla-c06",
        "selkolla-c08",
        "selkolla-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisahnya berlatar di Vestfirðir (Fjord Barat). Sepasang suami-istri yang membawa bayi perempuan untuk dibaptis berhenti di dekat batu besar Miklisteinn untuk bersenggama; ketika kembali, bayi itu sudah hitam dan mati, lalu hidup lagi dalam rupa yang begitu mengerikan sehingga mereka tak berani menyentuhnya, dan kemudian lenyap. Tak lama muncul di daerah itu seorang perempuan yang kadang berwajah biasa, kadang berkepala anjing laut, \"setan tengah hari\" yang berkeliaran siang dan malam.",
      "en": "The tale is set in the Westfjords. A couple taking a baby girl to be baptised stopped by the great stone Miklisteinn to have sex; when they returned the child was black and dead, then alive again but so frightful that they dared not touch it, and afterwards it vanished. Soon a woman appeared in the district, sometimes with an ordinary face and sometimes with a seal's head, a 'midday devil' who roamed by day as well as by night.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "selkolla-c02",

        "selkolla-c10",
        "selkolla-c12"

    {
      "id": "Dalam rupa istri seorang petani, Selkolla mencoba merayu petani itu, lalu menghalangi jalannya sehingga ia jatuh sakit dan kehilangan kejantanannya; ia juga mencungkil mata seorang kerabat yang menjaga petani itu. Uskup Guðmundur akhirnya mengusirnya dengan kata-kata \"far niðr, fjandi\" (\"turunlah, setan\") dan keesokan harinya dengan mendirikan tujuh salib.",
      "en": "Taking the shape of a farmer's wife, Selkolla tried to seduce him, then blocked his way so that he fell sick and lost his virility; she also burst out the eyes of a kinsman who watched over him.       "quote": "Selkolla (literally 'seal-head') is a supernatural being in Icelandic folklore. She is described as a fair woman that is sometimes seen having the head of a seal.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selkolla (harfiah \"kepala anjing laut\") adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Islandia (Icelandic), perempuan cantik yang kadang terlihat berkepala anjing laut; ia terutama tercatat sebagai lawan Uskup Guðmundur Arason (1161–1237), dan kisahnya memadukan gagasan Kristen (Christian) dengan konsep tradisi Skandinavia (Scandinavian) non-Kristen.",
        "en": "Selkolla ('seal-head') is a supernatural being of Icelandic folklore, a fair woman sometimes seen with a seal's head; she is chiefly attested as an antagonist of Bishop Guðmundur Arason (1161–1237), and her tales mix Christian ideas with Scandinavian non-Christian concepts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c02",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s1",
      "quote": "It is then said that a certain woman is \"newly arrived in the district, sometimes with an ordinary face but sometimes with a seal's head. This devil walked about as boldly by day as by night, for which reason this midday-devil was called Selkolla in the district\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lalu disebutkan seorang perempuan baru tiba di daerah itu, kadang berwajah biasa, kadang berkepala anjing laut; setan ini berkeliaran berani siang maupun malam, sehingga \"setan tengah hari\" ini disebut Selkolla.",
        "en": "Then a woman is said to have newly arrived in the district, sometimes with an ordinary face, sometimes with a seal's head; this devil walked boldly by day and night, so this midday-devil was called Selkolla."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c03",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s1",
      "quote": "The events of Selkollu þáttr take place in the Westfjords, beginning when a man and woman are taking a baby girl to be baptised.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peristiwa Selkollu þáttr terjadi di Fjord Barat (Westfjords), bermula ketika seorang lelaki dan perempuan membawa bayi perempuan untuk dibaptis.",
        "en": "The events of Selkollu þáttr take place in the Westfjords, beginning when a man and woman take a baby girl to be baptised."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c04",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s1",
      "quote": "First, in the guise of the farmer's wife, she tries to entice him into having sex. When he realises what is actually going on, he tries to turn for home, but Selkolla bars his way",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mula-mula, menyamar sebagai istri petani, ia mencoba merayunya; ketika petani menyadarinya dan hendak pulang, Selkolla menghalangi jalannya.",
        "en": "First, disguised as the farmer's wife, she tries to seduce him; when he realises and turns for home, Selkolla bars his way."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c05",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s1",
      "quote": "Guðmundur realises immediately who it is and drives Selkolla away with the words \"far niðr, fjandi, ok gakk ei framarr!\" (\"go down, devil, and don't come back!\").       "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guðmundur (Guðmundr) segera mengenalinya dan mengusirnya dengan kata \"far niðr, fjandi, ok gakk ei framarr!\" (\"turunlah, setan, dan jangan kembali!\"); keesokan harinya ia mengusirnya lagi dengan mendirikan tujuh salib.",
        "en": "Guðmundur (Guðmundr) knows her at once and drives her off with 'far niðr, fjandi, ok gakk ei framarr!' ('go down, devil, and don't come back!'); the next day he banishes her again by setting up seven crosses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c06",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s1",
      "quote": "The main medieval source for stories about Selkolla is different versions of Guðmundar saga Arasonar, specifically versions B and D, where the episode is often now referred to as Selkollu þáttr ('the Selkolla episode').",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber abad pertengahan utama kisah Selkolla adalah versi B dan D Guðmundar saga Arasonar, yang bagiannya kini sering disebut Selkollu þáttr (\"episode Selkolla\").",
        "en": "The main medieval sources on Selkolla are versions B and D of Guðmundar saga Arasonar, the episode now often called Selkollu þáttr ('the Selkolla episode')."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c07",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s2",
      "quote": "Selkolla er kynjavera í íslenskri þjóðtrú. Hún er falleg kona, en sést stundum með selshöfuð.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selkolla adalah makhluk ajaib dalam kepercayaan rakyat Islandia (íslenskri); ia perempuan cantik yang kadang terlihat berkepala anjing laut.",
        "en": "Selkolla is a wondrous being of Icelandic (íslenskri) folk belief; a beautiful woman, sometimes seen with a seal's head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c08",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s2",
      "quote": "Hennar er fyrst getið í Íslendinga sögu eftir Sturlu Þórðarson og er þar nefnt að biskupinn hafi glímt við veruna á Vestfjörðum.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selkolla pertama kali disebut dalam Íslendinga saga karya Sturla Þórðarson (Sturlu Þórðarson), yang menyatakan sang uskup bergulat dengan makhluk itu di Vestfirðir (Vestfjörðum).",
        "en": "She is first mentioned in Sturla Þórðarson's Íslendinga saga, which says the bishop struggled with the being in the Westfjords (Vestfjörðum)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c09",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s2",
      "quote": "Sagan af Selkollu þykir minna á seinni tíma þjóðsögur af huldubörnum og barnadraugum.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Selkolla dianggap mengingatkan pada cerita rakyat yang lebih kemudian tentang anak-anak huldufólk dan hantu anak.",
        "en": "The Selkolla story is thought to recall later folk tales of hidden-folk children and child ghosts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c10",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s1",
      "quote": "Once they have finished having sex, they return to the child, who they had set down, and find that it is now \"black, dead, and hideous\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selesai bersenggama, mereka kembali ke bayi yang mereka letakkan dan mendapatinya \"hitam, mati, dan mengerikan\"; bayi itu kemudian hidup lagi dengan rupa menakutkan lalu lenyap.",
        "en": "Having finished, they go back to the child they had set down and find it 'black, dead, and hideous'; it later revives in a terrifying form and vanishes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c11",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s1",
      "quote": "except for one diligent kinsman of his, who lies beside him until Selkolla overcomes him in the night and bursts out his eyes",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hanya seorang kerabat yang rajin menemaninya, sampai Selkolla mengalahkannya di malam hari dan mencungkil matanya; petani itu sendiri jatuh sakit dan kehilangan kejantanan.",
        "en": "Only a diligent kinsman stays with him, until Selkolla overcomes him at night and bursts out his eyes; the farmer himself is left sick and robbed of his virility."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "selkolla-c12",
      "source_id": "selkolla-s1",
      "quote": "On the way, they indulge in an \"immoral rest\" beside a large stone, fittingly called Miklisteinn ('large stone')",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam perjalanan mereka melakukan \"istirahat tak bermoral\" di samping batu besar yang dinamai Miklisteinn (\"batu besar\").",
        "en": "On the way they take an 'immoral rest' beside a large stone aptly named Miklisteinn ('large stone')."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Eckart yang Setia (der treue Eckart) adalah tokoh legenda heroik Jermanik dan cerita rakyat Jerman: wali para Harlung dalam kisah Dietrich von Bern, dan pemberi peringatan yang berjalan di depan Perburuan Liar.",
    "en": "Faithful Eckart (der treue Eckart) is a figure of Germanic heroic legend and German folklore: guardian of the Harlungs in the Dietrich von Bern legends, and the warner who walks ahead of the Wild Hunt.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "treuer-eckart-c01",
      "treuer-eckart-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam legenda heroik, Eckart (juga Eckehart atau Eckewart) adalah wali dua pemuda Harlung yang mewarisi Breisgau, Kastel Breisach, dan harta tak terhingga dari ayah mereka, Diether. Ia sendiri seorang Harlung dan termasyhur karena kesetiaannya. Ketika Raja Ermenrich, paman mereka, menghasut oleh penasihat Sibeche, merebut Breisach saat Eckart tak ada, menggantung kedua keponakannya dan merampas hartanya, Eckart membalas dendam: ia menyeret Sibeche ke tiang gantungan dalam pertempuran Rabenschlacht dan bersama para pahlawan lain membunuh Ermenrich.",
      "en": "In heroic legend, Eckart (also Eckehart or Eckewart) is the guardian of two young Harlungs who inherited the Breisgau, Breisach Castle and boundless treasure from their father Diether. He is himself a Harlung and famed for his loyalty. When King Ermenrich, their uncle, egged on by his adviser Sibeche, seized Breisach while Eckart was away, hanged his nephews and took their wealth, Eckart avenged them: he dragged Sibeche to the gallows during the Rabenschlacht and, with other heroes, killed Ermenrich.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "treuer-eckart-c02",
        "treuer-eckart-c03",
        "treuer-eckart-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Harta para Harlung konon disihir dan disembunyikan di gunung Bürglenberg dekat Breisach, dijaga arwah Eckart yang memperingatkan setiap pemburu harta bahwa harta itu ditakdirkan jatuh ke tangan ahli waris sah. Karena itulah orang Jerman punya pepatah \"Du bist der treue Eckart, du warnest jedermann\", yang dipakai untuk orang yang suka memperingatkan semua orang.",
      "en": "The Harlungs' treasure is said to be enchanted and hidden in the Bürglenberg near Breisach, guarded by Eckart's spirit, who tells every treasure-seeker that it is destined for the rightful heir. Hence the German proverb \"Du bist der treue Eckart, du warnest jedermann\", said of anyone who warns everybody.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "treuer-eckart-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Thüringen, Eckart berjalan di depan Perburuan Liar (wütendes Heer) yang dipimpin Frau Holle pada Dua Belas Malam Natal, menyuruh orang menyingkir agar tidak celaka. Ia digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua bertongkat putih. Saat rombongan itu tinggal di dalam gunung Hörselberg, Eckart duduk di depan guanya dan memperingatkan siapa pun agar tidak memasuki alam Frau Venus, tempat tiada jalan kembali; peran ini sudah dikenal sejak 1453.",
      "en": "In Thuringia, Eckart walks ahead of the Wild Hunt (wütendes Heer) led by Frau Holle during the Twelve Nights of Christmas, telling people to step aside so they come to no harm. He is pictured as an old man with a white staff. When the host stays inside the Hörselberg, Eckart sits before the cave warning everyone not to enter the realm of Frau Venus, from which there is no return; this role has been known since 1453.",

      "quote": "is a character of Germanic heroic legend, particularly the legends about Dietrich von Bern, and also features in German legends about the wild hunt.",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Eckart, biasa disebut der treue Eckart atau treuer Eckart (\"Eckart yang setia\"), adalah tokoh legenda heroik Jermanik (Germanic), khususnya legenda Dietrich von Bern, dan juga muncul dalam legenda Jerman (German) tentang Perburuan Liar (wild hunt).",
        "en": "Eckart, commonly der treue Eckart or treuer Eckart ('the faithful Eckart'), is a figure of Germanic heroic legend, especially the Dietrich von Bern legends, and also features in German legends of the Wild Hunt."

    {

      "quote": "In heroic legend, Eckart, also called Eckehart or Eckewart, is the guardian of the Harlungs, two young men whose late father Diether bequeathed them the Breisgau territory, Breisach Castle, and immeasurable treasure.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda heroik, Eckart (Eckehart, Eckewart) adalah wali para Harlung, dua pemuda yang mewarisi wilayah Breisgau, Kastel Breisach, dan harta tak terhingga dari mendiang ayah mereka Diether; ia sendiri Harlung dan termasyhur karena kesetiaannya; para Harlung adalah sepupu Dietrich von Bern.",

      "quote": "During the Rabenschlacht battle, Eckart took Sibeche captive and dragged him away on horseback to be hung on the gallows. He also took revenge for the demise of his charges when he and other heroes slew Ermenrich.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam pertempuran Rabenschlacht, Eckart menawan Sibeche dan menyeretnya dengan kuda ke tiang gantungan; ia membalas kematian anak-anak asuhnya dengan membunuh Ermenrich bersama pahlawan lain.",

      "quote": "The rich treasure of the Harlungs, the Harlungenhort, is said to be enchanted and hidden inside the mountain Bürglenberg near Breisach.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Harta Harlung (Harlungenhort) konon disihir dan disembunyikan di gunung Bürglenberg dekat Breisach, dijaga arwah Eckart yang memperingatkan setiap pemburu harta bahwa harta itu ditakdirkan bagi ahli waris sejati; dari sini lahir pepatah \"Du bist der treue Eckart, du warnest jedermann\".",
        "en": "The Harlungs' treasure (Harlungenhort) is said to be enchanted in the Bürglenberg near Breisach, guarded by Eckart's spirit, who warns every treasure-seeker that it is fated for the true heir; hence the saying 'Du bist der treue Eckart, du warnest jedermann'."

      "source_id": "treuer-eckart-s1",
      "quote": "In Thuringia, der treue Eckart is connected with the wild hunt, locally called wütendes Heer or furious host. Der treue Eckart goes ahead of the wild hunt, warning the people to step out of the way so they will suffer no harm.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Thüringen (Thuringia), Eckart terkait dengan Perburuan Liar (wild hunt; wütendes Heer) yang dipimpin Frau Holle pada Dua Belas Malam Natal (Twelve Nights of Christmas); ia berjalan di depannya, menyuruh orang menyingkir agar tidak celaka.",
        "en": "In Thuringia Eckart is tied to the Wild Hunt (wütendes Heer) led by Frau Holle over the Twelve Nights of Christmas; he goes ahead of it, telling people to step aside so they come to no harm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treuer-eckart-c06",
      "source_id": "treuer-eckart-s1",
      "quote": "His appearance is that of an old man with a staff. When the furious host doesn’t travel and stays inside Hörselberg Mountain, der treue Eckart sits outside the cave, warning everybody against entering the realm of Frau Venus from where there is no return.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berwujud lelaki tua bertongkat; saat rombongan itu tinggal di dalam gunung Hörselberg, Eckart duduk di luar gua memperingatkan semua orang agar tidak memasuki alam Frau Venus (Venusberg), tempat tiada jalan kembali.",
        "en": "He looks like an old man with a staff; when the host stays inside the Hörselberg, Eckart sits outside the cave warning all against entering the realm of Frau Venus (the Venusberg), whence none return."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treuer-eckart-c07",
      "source_id": "treuer-eckart-s2",
      "quote": "Der Getreue Eckart oder Treue Eckart (auch in den diversen Namensvarianten von „Eckart“ wie „Eckhart“, „Eckehard“ usw.) ist eine Figur mit Wurzeln in der germanischen Sagenwelt, zugleich ein Stoff der Literatur und Kunst.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Getreuer Eckart atau Treue Eckart adalah tokoh berakar dalam dunia legenda Jermanik dan juga bahan sastra serta seni.",
        "en": "The Getreue Eckart or Treue Eckart is a figure rooted in Germanic legend and also a subject of literature and art."
      }
    },

      "id": "treuer-eckart-c08",
      "source_id": "treuer-eckart-s1",
      "quote": "That der treue Eckart guards the entrance to the Venusberg, i.e. the mountain of Frau Venus, and advises people against entering is known since 1453 if not longer, giving rise to the saying mentioned above. In Denmark, he also is found warning people before entering a brothel, and a chronicle from 1566 places him as a judge at the gates of hell.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peran Eckart menjaga pintu Venusberg dan menasihati orang agar tidak masuk dikenal setidaknya sejak 1453; di Denmark ia memperingatkan orang sebelum memasuki rumah bordil, dan kronik tahun 1566 menempatkannya sebagai hakim di gerbang neraka.",
        "en": "Eckart's guarding of the Venusberg entrance and warning people off is known since at least 1453; in Denmark he warns people before entering a brothel, and a chronicle of 1566 makes him a judge at the gates of hell."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treuer-eckart-c09",
      "source_id": "treuer-eckart-s2",
      "quote": "Der Getreue Eckart macht sie ihnen wieder voll und verspricht, sie würden immer voll bleiben, solange die Kinder über das Geschaute Schweigen bewahrten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Eckart mengisi kembali kendi bir anak-anak yang diminum rombongan Perburuan Liar dan berjanji kendi itu akan selalu penuh selama mereka merahasiakan apa yang mereka lihat (Thüringen).",
        "en": "Eckart refilled the children's beer jugs drunk by the Wild Hunt and promised they would stay full as long as the children kept silent about what they had seen (Thüringen)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treuer-eckart-c10",
      "source_id": "treuer-eckart-s3",
      "quote": "Jörg Wickram använde Eckart i pjäsen Der trew Eckart från 1532. Johann Wolfgang Goethe gjorde honom till huvudperson i balladen Der getreue Eckart från 1813",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jörg Wickram memakai Eckart dalam lakon Der trew Eckart (1532), dan Johann Wolfgang Goethe menjadikannya tokoh utama balada Der getreue Eckart (1813).",
        "en": "Jörg Wickram used Eckart in the play Der trew Eckart (1532), and Johann Wolfgang Goethe made him the hero of the ballad Der getreue Eckart (1813)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treuer-eckart-c11",
      "source_id": "treuer-eckart-s1",
      "quote": "One day, when Eckart was absent, King Ermenrich, uncle of the Harlungs, driven by both greed for his nephews’ heritage and his advisor Sibeche’s slanderous, fraudulent remarks",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suatu hari saat Eckart tak ada, Raja Ermenrich, paman para Harlung, terdorong ketamakan akan warisan keponakannya dan fitnah penasihatnya Sibeche, merebut Kastel Breisach, menggantung keponakannya, dan merampas harta mereka.",
        "en": "One day while Eckart was away, King Ermenrich, the Harlungs' uncle, driven by greed for their inheritance and his adviser Sibeche's slanders, seized Breisach Castle, hanged his nephews and took their property."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

    }
  ]
}

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Burung Gereja Ulm (Ulmer Spatz) adalah lambang kota Ulm di Jerman, berasal dari legenda jenaka tentang burung gereja yang mengajari warga cara memasukkan balok panjang melalui gerbang kota.",
    "en": "The Ulm Sparrow (Ulmer Spatz) is the emblem of the German city of Ulm, rooted in a humorous legend about a sparrow that taught the townsfolk how to get a long beam through the city gate.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ulm-sparrow-c01",
      "ulm-sparrow-c02",
      "ulm-sparrow-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Burung Gereja Ulm adalah tengara dan lambang kota Ulm: patung burung yang bertengger di atap Katedral Ulm (Ulmer Münster). Menurut legenda, warga Ulm yang membangun katedral itu tak bisa memasukkan sebuah balok yang sangat besar melalui gerbang kota. Saat hendak merobohkan gerbang, mereka melihat seekor burung gereja membawa ranting untuk sarangnya dan memutarnya memanjang; mereka pun meletakkan balok itu memanjang di gerobak, bukan melintang.",
      "en": "The Ulm Sparrow is a landmark and emblem of the city of Ulm: a bird statue perched on the roof of Ulm Minster. Legend tells that while building the minster, the townsfolk could not fit an especially large beam through their gate. Just before demolishing the gate, they spotted a sparrow turning a nesting twig lengthwise in its beak; so they loaded the beam lengthwise on their cart instead of across it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ulm-sparrow-c01",
        "ulm-sparrow-c02",
        "ulm-sparrow-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda jenaka ini baru dapat dilacak sejak perempat kedua abad ke-19 dan termasuk dalam kelompok kisah yang tersebar luas tentang batang kayu yang diangkut melintang, yang masalahnya dipecahkan dengan mengamati burung membangun sarang. Carl Hertzog sudah menerbitkan puisi \"Der Ulmer Spatz. Volkssage\" pada 1842, dan pada 1872 Eduard Mauch menyebut legenda itu \"pemalsuan sejarah\".",
      "en": "This humorous legend can only be traced back to the second quarter of the 19th century and belongs to a widespread cycle of tales about logs carried crosswise, the problem being solved by watching a bird build its nest. Carl Hertzog published the poem 'Der Ulmer Spatz. Volkssage' in 1842, and in 1872 Eduard Mauch called the legend a 'falsification of history'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ulm-sparrow-c04",
        "ulm-sparrow-c05",
        "ulm-sparrow-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sosok di atap katedral sebenarnya bukan burung gereja, melainkan merpati dengan ranting zaitun di paruhnya seperti dalam kisah Bahtera Nuh, sumbangan warga kaya; ukurannya kecil dibanding bangunan dan hanya mudah terlihat dari menara. Patung asli tahun 1858 kini disimpan dalam lemari kaca di dekat pintu masuk katedral.",
      "en": "The figure on the minster roof is in fact not a sparrow but a dove with an olive branch in its beak, as in the story of Noah's Ark, donated by wealthy citizens; it is small next to the building and easy to see only from the tower. The original statue of 1858 is now kept in a display case near the minster entrance.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ulm-sparrow-c07",
        "ulm-sparrow-c08"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "The Ulm Sparrow (Ger. Ulmer Spatz) is a landmark in, and symbol of, the German city of Ulm. The landmark is a statue of a bird that sits atop the Ulm Minster, the second-tallest church in the world.",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Burung Gereja Ulm (Ulmer Spatz) adalah tengara dan lambang kota Ulm di Jerman (German), berupa patung burung di atas Katedral Ulm (Ulm Minster), gereja tertinggi kedua di dunia.",

      "source_id": "ulm-sparrow-s2",
      "quote": "Der Sage nach sollen die Ulmer beim Bau des Münsters einen besonders großen Balken angekarrt haben. Sie schafften es aber nicht, ihn durch das Stadttor zu bringen.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, warga Ulm membawa balok sangat besar untuk membangun katedral (Münsters) tetapi tak bisa memasukkannya lewat gerbang kota; saat hendak merobohkan gerbang, mereka melihat burung gereja membawa ranting untuk sarangnya.",
        "en": "Legend says the people of Ulm carted a huge beam for building the minster (Münsters) but could not get it through the city gate; about to tear the gate down, they saw a sparrow carrying a twig for its nest."

      "source_id": "ulm-sparrow-s2",
      "quote": "Dieser Spatz drehte den Zweig vor dem Einbau längsseits. Da ging dann wohl auch den Ulmern ein Licht auf und sie legten den Balken der Länge nach auf ihren Karren und nicht quer, wie bisher.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Burung gereja itu memutar rantingnya memanjang sebelum menyusunnya; warga Ulm pun tersadar dan meletakkan balok memanjang di gerobak, bukan melintang seperti sebelumnya; Ulmer Spatz adalah lambang (Wahrzeichen) Ulm.",
        "en": "The sparrow turned the twig lengthwise before building it in; the Ulmers then saw the light and laid the beam lengthwise on their cart rather than across as before; the Ulmer Spatz is a landmark (Wahrzeichen) of Ulm."

      "quote": "Sie gehört zu dem weit verbreiteten Sagenkreis um kreuzweis statt längsseits transportierte Baumstämme und Holzbalken, wobei die Lösung des Problems durch die Beobachtung eines Vogels (Sperling, Storch, Dohle etc.) beim Nestbau gewonnen wurde.       "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini termasuk kelompok kisah yang tersebar luas tentang batang kayu dan balok yang diangkut melintang, dipecahkan dengan mengamati burung (burung gereja, bangau, gagak) membangun sarang; pada 1872 Eduard Mauch menyebutnya \"pemalsuan sejarah\" (Geschichtsfälschung).",
        "en": "It belongs to a widespread cycle of tales about logs and beams carried crosswise, solved by watching a bird (sparrow, stork, jackdaw) build its nest; in 1872 Eduard Mauch called it a 'falsification of history' (Geschichtsfälschung)."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Csodaszarvas (\"rusa ajaib\") adalah hewan pemandu mitis dalam siklus legenda Hun-Hungaria, yang menuntun Hunor dan Magor, leluhur bangsa Hun dan Magyar, ke tanah air baru.",
    "en": "The Csodaszarvas ('wondrous stag') is a mythical guiding animal in the Hun-Hungarian legend cycle, who led Hunor and Magor, ancestors of the Huns and Magyars, to a new homeland.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "white-stag-c01",
      "white-stag-c03",
      "white-stag-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Csodaszarvas adalah hewan dongeng dalam siklus legenda Hun dan Hungaria, hewan pemandu mitis yang dikirim Tuhan menurut tradisi rakyat Hungaria. Legendanya terlestarikan dalam Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum karya penulis kronik abad pertengahan Simon Kézai: ketika berburu, Hunor dan Magor, putra Ménrót dan Eneth, mengejar seekor rusa betina ke rawa-rawa Meotis, tempat hewan itu lenyap; di sana mereka menemukan tanah yang cocok untuk beternak.",
      "en": "The Csodaszarvas is a fabulous beast of the Hun and Hungarian legend cycle, a mythical guiding animal sent by God according to Hungarian folk tradition. Its legend survives in the Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum by the medieval chronicler Simon Kézai: while hunting, Hunor and Magor, sons of Ménrót and Eneth, chased a hind into the Meotis marshes, where it vanished; there they found land fit for raising cattle.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "white-stag-c01",
        "white-stag-c02",
        "white-stag-c03",
        "white-stag-c06",
        "white-stag-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penelitian menunjukkan kesejajaran motif ini di Timur dan Barat. Diduga bangsa Magyar membawa legenda bertipe timur ini saat menaklukkan tanah air mereka, dan kemudian legenda itu bercampur dengan legenda Kristen tentang Eustachius dan Hubertus. Bagi banyak bangsa Eurasia dan beberapa suku Amerika Utara, rusa ajaib sejak lama juga melambangkan langit berbintang.",
      "en": "Research has found parallels to the motif in both East and West. The Magyars are thought to have brought this eastern-type legend with them at the conquest of their homeland, after which it merged with the Christian legends of Eustace and Hubert. For many Eurasian peoples and some North American tribes, the wondrous stag has also long stood for the starry sky.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "white-stag-c04",
        "white-stag-c10",
        "white-stag-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Rusa ajaib melambangkan kelahiran kembali, pembaruan, dan matahari. Ia juga berperan sebagai hewan penunjuk jalan dalam Gesta Hungarorum anonim, tempat kepala suku Bors mengejar seekor rusa ke puncak gunung dan mendirikan bentengnya di sana. Dalam neopaganisme dan folklor Hungaria, rusa ini dianggap lambang nasional.",
      "en": "The wondrous stag symbolises rebirth, renewal and the sun. It also serves as a path-showing animal in the anonymous Gesta Hungarorum, where the chieftain Bors chases a stag up to the mountain tops and builds his castle there. In Hungarian neo-paganism and folklore the stag is regarded as a national symbol.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "white-stag-c05",
        "white-stag-c07",
        "white-stag-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

  },
  "did_you_know": {
    "id": "Menurut legenda, Hunor dan Magor menemukan tanah air baru bangsa Hun dan Magyar karena mengejar seekor rusa yang lenyap di rawa-rawa Meotis.",
    "en": "According to legend, Hunor and Magor found the new homeland of the Huns and Magyars by chasing a stag that vanished in the Meotis marshes.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "white-stag-c03"
    ]
  },
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [
    {

      "relation_type": "associated",

      },

  "modern_depictions": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "white-stag-s1",
      "url": "https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wunderhirsch",

    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-s2",
      "url": "https://hu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Csodaszarvas",
      "title": "Csodaszarvas",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (hu)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "hu",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c01",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "Der Wunderhirsch ist ein Fabeltier im hunnischen und ungarischen Sagenkreis.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wunderhirsch (rusa ajaib) adalah hewan dongeng dalam siklus legenda Hun dan Hungaria (ungarischen).",
        "en": "The Wunderhirsch (wondrous stag) is a fabulous beast in the Hun and Hungarian (ungarischen) legend cycle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c02",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "Im Werk Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum des mittelalterlichen Chronisten Simon Kézai blieb die Sage erhalten. Die Söhne von Ménrót und Eneth, Hunor und Magor, von denen der Legende nach die Hunnen und Magyaren abstammen, wurden in der Sage vom Wunderhirsch in ein neues Siedlungsgebiet geführt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini terlestarikan dalam Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum karya penulis kronik abad pertengahan Simon Kézai; Hunor dan Magor, putra Ménrót dan Eneth, leluhur bangsa Hun dan Magyar, dituntun rusa ajaib ke wilayah permukiman baru.",
        "en": "The legend survives in the Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum by medieval chronicler Simon Kézai; Hunor and Magor, sons of Ménrót and Eneth and ancestors of the Huns and Magyars, were led by the wondrous stag to a new land of settlement."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c03",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "Als sie einmal zum Jagen weggingen, ereignete es sich, dass sie in einer Steppe eine Hirschkuh angriffen, der sie, wie sie vor ihnen herlief, in das Sumpfgebiet Meotis nachjagten. Und nachdem er dort vor ihren Augen völlig verschwand, suchten sie lange, doch gelang es ihnen nicht, sie zu finden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat berburu di stepa, mereka menyerang seekor rusa betina dan mengejarnya ke rawa-rawa Meotis, tempat hewan itu lenyap dari pandangan; mereka lama mencarinya tanpa hasil.",
        "en": "Hunting on the steppe, they attacked a hind and chased it into the Meotis marshes, where it vanished from sight; they searched long in vain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c04",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "Es ist zu vermuten, dass die Ungarn zur Zeit der Landnahme die aus östlichen Kulturen stammende Sage mit sich brachten. Später vermischte sie sich mit den christlichen Legenden von Eustachius und Hubertus.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Diduga bangsa Hungaria (Ungarn) membawa legenda dari budaya timur ini saat penaklukan tanah air (Landnahme); kemudian legenda itu bercampur dengan legenda Kristen tentang Eustachius dan Hubertus.",
        "en": "The Hungarians (Ungarn) presumably brought this legend from eastern cultures at the time of the Landnahme; later it merged with the Christian legends of Eustace (Eustachius) and Hubert (Hubertus)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c05",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "Er kommt auch im ungarischen Neuheidentum bzw. in der (ggf. völkisch missbrauchten) Folklore als csodaszarvas vor und gilt als nationales Symbol.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rusa ajaib juga muncul dalam neopaganisme Hungaria dan folklor sebagai csodaszarvas dan dianggap lambang nasional.",
        "en": "It also appears in Hungarian neo-paganism and folklore as the csodaszarvas and is regarded as a national symbol."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c06",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s2",
      "quote": "A csodaszarvas a hun-magyar mondakörben és a magyar népi hagyományokban ismert, Isten által küldött mitikus vezérállat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "A csodaszarvas (rusa ajaib) adalah mitikus vezérállat, hewan pemandu mitis kiriman Isten (Tuhan), dikenal dalam hun-magyar mondakör (siklus legenda Hun-Hungaria) dan magyar népi hagyomány (tradisi rakyat Hungaria).",
        "en": "A csodaszarvas (wondrous stag) is a mitikus vezérállat, a mythical guiding animal sent by Isten (God), known in the hun-magyar mondakör (Hun-Hungarian legend cycle) and magyar népi hagyomány (Hungarian folk tradition)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c07",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s2",
      "quote": "A csodaszarvas az újjászületés és a megújulás, valamint a Nap jelképe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Csodaszarvas adalah lambang kelahiran kembali, pembaruan, dan Matahari (Nap).",
        "en": "The csodaszarvas is a symbol of rebirth, renewal and the Sun (Nap)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c08",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "Der Wunderhirsch spielt auch in den anonymen Gesta Hungarorum im Kapitel über den Stammesführer Bars und der Gründung seiner Burg eine Rolle als den Weg weisendes Tier.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rusa ajaib juga berperan sebagai hewan penunjuk jalan dalam Gesta Hungarorum anonim, pada bab tentang kepala suku Bars (Bors) dan pendirian bentengnya.",
        "en": "The wondrous stag also features as a path-showing animal in the anonymous Gesta Hungarorum, in the chapter on chieftain Bars (Bors) and the founding of his castle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c09",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "Schließlich fanden sie am Eingang des erwähnten Sumpfgebietes für Rinderhaltung geeignetes Land vor.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akhirnya, di tepi rawa-rawa itu mereka menemukan tanah yang cocok untuk beternak sapi.",
        "en": "At last, at the edge of those marshes, they found land fit for keeping cattle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c10",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "Der Wunderhirsch ist auch seit alten Zeiten ein Himmelszeichen bei vielen eurasischen Völkern und einigen nordamerikanischen Stämmen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak lama rusa ajaib menjadi lambang langit bagi banyak bangsa Eurasia (eurasischen) dan beberapa suku Amerika Utara (nordamerikanischen).",
        "en": "Since ancient times the wondrous stag has been a sky sign for many Eurasian peoples and some North American tribes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-stag-c11",
      "source_id": "white-stag-s1",
      "quote": "In der Forschung konnten Parallelen der weit verbreiteten Motivgruppe im Osten und Westen nachgewiesen werden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penelitian telah menunjukkan kesejajaran kelompok motif yang tersebar luas ini di Timur dan Barat.",
        "en": "Research has shown parallels of this widespread motif group in East and West."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Artikel sr adalah terjemahan artikel hu, sehingga tidak dipakai sebagai sumber tersendiri."
    },
    {

    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Airón adalah dewa pra-Romawi Semenanjung Iberia yang dikaitkan dengan sumur, mata air, dan perairan dalam, sekaligus dewa kehidupan dan dewa dunia bawah serta kematian.",
    "en": "Airón was a pre-Roman god of the Iberian Peninsula linked with wells, springs and deep waters, at once a god of life and a god of the underworld and death.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "airon-c01",
      "airon-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Airón adalah dewa pra-Romawi yang dipuja di Semenanjung Iberia dan sebagian Galia; kultusnya tetap dihormati oleh bangsa Romawi. Ia terkait dengan perairan dalam di sumur dan laguna serta dengan jurang, sehingga berhubungan langsung dengan dunia bawah.",
      "en": "Airón was a pre-Roman deity worshipped on the Iberian Peninsula and in parts of Gaul; his cult was respected by the Romans. He was tied to deep waters in wells and lagoons and to chasms, and so directly to the underworld.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "airon-c01",
        "airon-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagai dewa dunia bawah ia bermuka dua. Di sisi baiknya ia dewa kehidupan, karena dari dunia bawah muncul air dan tumbuhan; di sisi buruknya ia dewa kematian, karena dunia bawah adalah tujuan jiwa orang mati. Sumur yang tak pernah kering atau laguna yang tak terduga dalamnya dimaknai sebagai saluran yang menghubungkan dunia orang mati dengan bumi dan langit.",
      "en": "As an underworld god he had two faces. On the good side he was a god of life, since water and vegetation rise from the underworld; on the bad side a god of death, since the underworld was where the souls of the dead went. The never-failing well or bottomless lagoon was read as a channel linking the world of the dead with the earth and the sky.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "airon-c02",
        "airon-c04",
        "airon-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tidak ada patung dewa ini yang diketahui; yang dihormati hanyalah mata air dan sumur. Di Spanyol dikenal sekitar seratus nama tempat \"Pozo Airón\", ditambah beberapa di Prancis dan Portugal, dan di banyak tempat itu beredar legenda seram tentang orang yang tewas karena jatuh ke dalam sumur Airón.",
      "en": "No images of the god are known; only springs and wells were venerated. Around a hundred 'Pozo Airón' place names are known in Spain, with more in France and Portugal, and at many of them eerie legends tell of someone who dies by falling into the Airón well.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "airon-c05",
        "airon-c06",
        "airon-c07"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "airon-c01",
      "source_id": "airon-s1",
      "quote": "Airón war eine prärömische Gottheit, die auf der Iberischen Halbinsel und in Teilen Galliens verehrt wurde.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Airón adalah dewa pra-Romawi yang dipuja di Semenanjung Iberia (Iberischen) dan sebagian Galia (Galliens).",
        "en": "Airón was a pre-Roman deity worshipped on the Iberian Peninsula and in parts of Gaul (Galliens)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airon-c02",
      "source_id": "airon-s1",
      "quote": "Airón war verbunden mit Quelltöpfen und Brunnen und wurde insofern als Gott des Lebens verehrt. Gleichzeitig galt er aber auch als Gott der Unterwelt und des Todes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Airón terkait dengan mata air dan sumur sehingga dipuja sebagai dewa kehidupan, tetapi sekaligus dianggap dewa dunia bawah dan kematian.",
        "en": "Airón was connected with spring pools and wells and so revered as a god of life, yet also held to be a god of the underworld and death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airon-c03",
      "source_id": "airon-s2",
      "quote": "Airón fue un dios indígena que estaba arraigado en Hispania antes de que los romanos emprendieran su conquista. Este dios, cuyo culto fue respetado por los romanos, se relaciona con aguas profundas (ubicadas en pozos y lagunas) y también con simas, por lo tanto hay una relación directa entre Airón y el inframundo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Airón adalah dewa pribumi Hispania sebelum penaklukan Romawi (romanos); kultusnya dihormati bangsa Romawi, dan ia terkait dengan perairan dalam di sumur dan laguna serta jurang, sehingga berhubungan langsung dengan dunia bawah.",
        "en": "Airón was a native god rooted in Hispania before the Roman (romanos) conquest; his cult was respected by the Romans, and he relates to deep waters in wells and lagoons and to chasms, hence a direct link with the underworld."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airon-c04",
      "source_id": "airon-s2",
      "quote": "En su aspecto positivo Airón es el dios de la vida, pues del inframundo emerge el agua (fuente de vida) y la vegetación. En su vertiente negativa, Airón se nos manifiesta como el dios de la muerte, pues el inframundo era el lugar a donde se consideraba —en las religiones precristianas— que iban a parar las almas de los muertos",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam aspek positifnya Airón dewa kehidupan, karena dari dunia bawah muncul air dan tumbuhan; dalam aspek negatifnya ia dewa kematian, karena dunia bawah dianggap tujuan jiwa orang mati.",
        "en": "In his positive aspect Airón is the god of life, as water and vegetation emerge from the underworld; in his negative aspect the god of death, as the underworld was where souls of the dead were thought to go."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airon-c05",
      "source_id": "airon-s1",
      "quote": "Figürliche Darstellungen des Gottes sind nicht bekannt; verehrt wurden lediglich Quellen und Brunnen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tidak diketahui ada gambaran figuratif dewa ini; yang dipuja hanyalah mata air dan sumur.",
        "en": "No figurative images of the god are known; only springs and wells were revered."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airon-c06",
      "source_id": "airon-s2",
      "quote": "En España se conocen ciento un topónimos Airón, a los que se han de añadir seis hallados en Francia, ocho en Portugal, uno en Italia, uno en el País de Gales, uno en México y otro en Brasil.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Spanyol (España) dikenal 101 nama tempat (topónimos) Airón seperti \"Pozo Airón\", ditambah enam di Prancis (Francia), delapan di Portugal, dan masing-masing satu di Italia, Wales, Meksiko, dan Brasil.",
        "en": "Spain (España) has 101 Airón place names, plus six in France (Francia), eight in Portugal, and one each in Italy, Wales, Mexico and Brazil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airon-c07",
      "source_id": "airon-s2",
      "quote": "aunque en muchos sitios donde existe un pozo Airón se encuentran leyendas de terror, en las que el protagonista termina muriendo al caer en el pozo Airón.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di banyak tempat yang memiliki sumur Airón beredar legenda seram yang tokohnya tewas karena jatuh ke dalam sumur Airón.",
        "en": "At many sites with an Airón well there are horror legends whose protagonist dies by falling into the Airón well."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airon-c08",
      "source_id": "airon-s2",
      "quote": "El pozo de agua inagotable o la laguna insondable en realidad son una metáfora que significa el canal que comunica el inframundo —o mundo de los muertos de donde no se puede salir— con la tierra habitada por los hombres y con el cielo donde se suponía que moraban los dioses.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumur yang airnya tak habis atau laguna tak terduga dalamnya sebenarnya metafora saluran yang menghubungkan dunia bawah (dunia orang mati) dengan bumi tempat manusia dan langit tempat para dewa.",
        "en": "The inexhaustible well or unfathomable lagoon is really a metaphor for the channel joining the underworld, the world of the dead, with the earth of humans and the sky of the gods."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Árvakr (\"bangun pagi\") adalah salah satu dari dua kuda dalam mitologi Nordik, bersama Alsviðr, yang setiap hari menarik kereta matahari Sól melintasi langit.",
    "en": "Árvakr ('early awake') is one of the two horses of Norse mythology, together with Alsviðr, that pull Sól's sun chariot across the sky each day.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "arvakr-c01",
      "arvakr-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Árvakr (\"bangun pagi\") dan Alsviðr (\"sangat cepat\") adalah kuda-kuda yang setiap hari menarik matahari, atau kereta Sól, melintasi langit. Keduanya disebut dalam Gylfaginning dan Grímnismál, dan nama mereka kerap dikaitkan dengan gambaran matahari.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Árvakr ('early awake') and Alsviðr ('very quick') are the horses that draw the sun, or Sól's chariot, across the sky every day. Both appear in Gylfaginning and Grímnismál, and their names are often linked with descriptions of the Sun.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "arvakr-c01",
        "arvakr-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut mitosnya, para dewa membentuk matahari dari percikan api Muspelheim, tetapi matahari diam tanpa pengemudi, maka Sól diculik untuk mengemudikannya dalam kereta yang ditarik dua kuda. Untuk melindungi kuda-kuda itu dari panas matahari yang luar biasa, para dewa memasang dua puputan (ísarnkol, \"besi dingin\") di bawah bahu mereka. Sól tak boleh berhenti, sebab serigala Sköll akan menangkap dan menelan matahari, seperti yang diperkirakan terjadi pada hari Ragnarök.",
      "en": "According to the myth, the gods shaped the Sun from a spark of Muspelheim's flame, but it stood still without a driver, so Sól was abducted to drive it in a chariot drawn by two horses. To shield the horses from the Sun's tremendous heat, the gods set two bellows (ísarnkol, 'cold iron') beneath their shoulders. Sól cannot stop, for the wolf Sköll would catch and swallow the Sun, as is expected to happen on the day of Ragnarök.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "arvakr-c03",
        "arvakr-c04",
        "arvakr-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Motif matahari yang ditarik kuda tidak khas Nordik atau Jermanik; banyak mitologi lain memiliki dewa matahari atau kereta matahari yang ditarik kuda. Mitos Árvakr dan Alsviðr diduga mengilhami lakon The Triumph of Peace (1663) karya dramawan Inggris James Shirley.",
      "en": "The motif of a horse-drawn sun is not unique to Norse or Germanic religion; many other mythologies have a sun god or sun chariot drawn by horses. English playwright James Shirley's The Triumph of Peace (1663) may have drawn on the story of these two horses.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "arvakr-c05",
        "arvakr-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "id": "Ashaqlun adalah setan dalam ajaran Manikheisme, putra Raja Kegelapan, yang bersama istrinya menciptakan pasangan manusia pertama, Adam dan Hawa.",
    "en": "Ashaqlun is a demon of Manichaean teaching, son of the King of Darkness, who with his consort created the first human pair, Adam and Eve.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ashaqlun (juga ditulis Ashaqlūn) adalah setan Manikheisme yang disebut dalam Apokalipsis Adam dalam kaitannya dengan penciptaan Adam dan Hawa. Ia putra Raja Kegelapan, dan istrinya bernama Namrael atau Nebroel; konon merekalah yang menciptakan pasangan manusia pertama, Gehmurd (manusia pertama, Adam) dan Murdiyanag (ibu kehidupan, Hawa).",
      "en": "Ashaqlun (also written Ashaqlūn) is a Manichaean demon named in the Apocalypse of Adam in connection with the creation of Adam and Eve. He is the son of the King of Darkness, and his consort is Namrael or Nebroel; they are said to have created the first human pair, Gehmurd (the first man, Adam) and Murdiyanag (mother of life, Eve).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ashaqlun-c01",
        "ashaqlun-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ashaqlun meminta para makhluk cacat menyerahkan putra dan putri mereka serta sebagian cahaya yang telah mereka rampas, dengan janji akan membuatkan wujud seperti \"manusia pertama\" yang pernah mereka lihat. Ia lalu memakan anak-anak lelaki dan memberikan anak-anak perempuan kepada istrinya, untuk memakan \"cahaya kehidupan\" mereka; setelah keduanya bersatu, Nebroel melahirkan seorang putra yang dinamai Adam, lalu seorang putri yang dinamai Hawa.",
      "en": "Ashaqlun asked the misshapen beings to hand over their sons and daughters and some of the light they had seized, promising to make for them a form like the 'first man' they had seen. He then devoured the boys and gave the girls to his consort, to feed on their 'life-lights'; after the two united, Nebroel bore a son named Adam, and then a daughter named Eve.",

        "ashaqlun-c04"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "Ashaqlun ist der Sohn des Königs der Finsternis. Seine Gemahlin ist Namrael oder Nebroel. Angeblich erschufen sie das erste Menschenpaar, Gehmurd (Erster Mensch=Adam) und Murdiyanag (Mutter des Lebens=Eva).",

      "id": "ashaqlun-c03",
      "source_id": "ashaqlun-s1",
      "quote": "“Bring me your sons and daughters; come, give me some of the Light we have taken; it is I who will make for you a form like what you have seen, which is the first man (not the heavenly first man).”",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ashaqlun berkata: bawalah kepadaku putra-putri kalian dan berikan sebagian cahaya (Light) yang telah kita ambil; akulah yang akan membuatkan wujud seperti yang telah kalian lihat, yaitu manusia pertama (first man).",

    {
      "id": "ashaqlun-c04",
      "source_id": "ashaqlun-s1",
      "quote": "Nun brachten sie ihre Kinder zu ihm und er verspeiste die Knaben und gab die Mädchen seiner Gemahlin, um sich an deren „Lebenslichtern“ zu nähren. Anschließend vereinigten sich die beiden und Nebroel wurde schwanger. Sie gebar einen Sohn, dem sie den Namen „Adam“ gab.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka membawa anak-anaknya kepadanya; ia memakan anak lelaki dan memberikan anak perempuan kepada istrinya untuk memakan \"cahaya kehidupan\" (Lebenslichtern) mereka; lalu keduanya bersatu, Nebroel hamil dan melahirkan putra bernama Adam, kemudian putri bernama Hawa (Eva).",
        "en": "They brought him their children; he ate the boys and gave the girls to his consort to feed on their 'life-lights' (Lebenslichtern); then the two united, Nebroel conceived and bore a son named Adam, and later a daughter named Eve (Eva)."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Aufhocker atau Huckup adalah makhluk pengubah wujud dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang melompat ke punggung pejalan malam dan makin berat di setiap langkah.",
    "en": "The Aufhocker or Huckup is a shapeshifter of German folklore that leaps onto the backs of night travellers and grows heavier with every step.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "aufhocker-c01",
      "aufhocker-c03",
      "aufhocker-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Aufhocker (bahasa Jerman Hilir Huckup) adalah makhluk pengubah wujud dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang tak punya bentuk tetap: ia bisa tampil sebagai goblin, perempuan cantik, orang mati, atau berbagai hewan seperti beruang, anak sapi, manusia serigala, atau anjing. Namanya berarti \"yang melompat ke atas\", sebab ia melompat ke punggung korbannya, sering pencuri dan penjahat yang perlu diberi pelajaran, pejalan sendirian, atau perempuan tua.",
      "en": "The Aufhocker (Low German Huckup) is a shapeshifter of German folklore with no fixed form: goblin, lovely woman, corpse, or a beast such as a bear, calf, werewolf or dog are all among its guises. Its name means 'leap upon', because it springs onto its victims' backs, often thieves and wrongdoers who need a lesson, lone travellers or old women.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aufhocker-c01",
        "aufhocker-c02",
        "aufhocker-c03",
        "aufhocker-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagai roh penekan, ia duduk di bahu pejalan malam dan makin berat di setiap langkah, sehingga korbannya lumpuh, sesak, dan tak bisa menoleh; banyak yang mati kelelahan karena ia tak bisa dienyahkan. Korban dapat bebas bila berhasil sampai di rumah, atau bila datang cahaya fajar, doa, atau dentang lonceng. Serangan ini terjadi di tempat tertentu: jembatan, persimpangan, mata air, hutan, jalan cekung, dan pemakaman.",
      "en": "As a pressure spirit it sits on a night walker's shoulders and grows heavier at each step, leaving the victim paralysed, oppressed and unable to turn around; many die of exhaustion because it cannot be shaken off. A victim can be freed by reaching home, or by dawn light, a prayer or the ringing of bells. Such attacks happen at particular places: bridges, crossroads, springs, woods, sunken lanes and cemeteries.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aufhocker-c04",
        "aufhocker-c06",
        "aufhocker-c08",
        "aufhocker-c13"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kepercayaan akan Aufhocker berakar pada ketakutan terhadap mayat hidup (Wiedergänger): laporan tertua berbicara tentang \"mayat yang melompat ke punggung\", bukan kobold atau hantu. Sebagian versi menyebut ia merobek leher korban, yang dikaitkan dengan vampirisme. Mitos serupa ada di banyak bagian Eropa, seperti Ganipote di Charente-Maritime, Prancis.",
      "en": "Belief in the Aufhocker is rooted in fear of the revenant (Wiedergänger): the oldest reports speak of 'corpses that leap onto one's back', not of kobolds or ghosts. Some versions say it tears out its victims' throats, which links it with vampirism. Similar myths exist across much of Europe, such as the Ganipote of Charente-Maritime in France.",
