```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Geldmännlein (\"lelaki kecil uang\") adalah roh rumah mirip kobold dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang secara gaib melipatgandakan uang pemiliknya.",
    "en": "The Geldmännlein ('little money man') is a kobold-like house spirit of German folklore that magically multiplies its owner's money.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "geldmannlein-c01",
      "geldmannlein-c12"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Geldmännlein adalah makhluk mirip kobold dalam mitologi rakyat Jerman, roh rumah yang memasok uang bagi pemiliknya. Ia tidak selalu berwujud lelaki kecil; ia bisa tampil sebagai kodok, katak, burung hantu, elang, cacing, atau kumbang. Sebutannya berbeda menurut daerah: Geldmännlein khas di Sachsen dan Thüringen, Geldschisser di Bayern, dan Alraunel di Austria Hilir.",
      "en": "The Geldmännlein is a kobold-like being of German folk mythology, a house spirit that supplies its owner with money. It does not always look like a little man; it may appear as a toad, frog, owl, eagle, worm or beetle. Its name varies by region: Geldmännlein is typical of Saxony and Thuringia, Geldschisser of Bavaria, and Alraunel of Lower Austria.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "geldmannlein-c01",
        "geldmannlein-c06",
        "geldmannlein-c09",
        "geldmannlein-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia disimpan, sesuai wujud dan ukurannya, dalam peti, kotak, atau periuk tempat orang menaruh uang pada malam hari; paginya uang yang telah \"dierami\" diambil, tetapi tak pernah semuanya agar sisanya terus berlipat. Seperti roh rumah lain, ia diberi sesaji susu, roti, atau bubur, dan bisa marah serta membalas dendam bila makanannya tak berkenan.",
      "en": "Depending on its form and size, it was kept in a chest, box or pot into which money was laid in the evening; next morning the 'hatched' money was taken out, but never all of it, so the rest could keep multiplying. Like other house spirits it received offerings of milk, bread or porridge, and could grow angry and take revenge if the food did not please it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "geldmannlein-c04",
        "geldmannlein-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penyebutan pertamanya tercatat pada 1657 dalam berkas pengadilan terhadap Caspar Michel Fuchs dari Sulzfeld dekat Meiningen, yang dituduh memiliki \"geldtmännlein\". Ia juga sangat sulit disingkirkan: dua pemilik pertama boleh menjualnya, tetapi jiwa pemilik ketiga jatuh ke neraka. Martin Luther sudah menolak kepercayaan semacam ini dalam Tischreden-nya.",
      "en": "Its first recorded mention is in 1657, in court records against Caspar Michel Fuchs of Sulzfeld near Meiningen, accused of owning a 'geldtmännlein'. It is also almost impossible to get rid of: the first two owners may sell it, but the soul of the third falls to hell. Martin Luther had already rejected such beliefs in his Tischreden.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "geldmannlein-c03",
        "geldmannlein-c05",
        "geldmannlein-c08",
        "geldmannlein-c11"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c01",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s1",
      "quote": "Das Geldmännlein ist ein koboldartiges Wesen der „Niederen Mythologie“, ein Hausgeist, der seinen Eigentümer mit Geld versorgt. Nicht immer hat es die Gestalt eines kleinen Männchens, sondern kann auch als Tier erscheinen, wie z. B. als Kröte oder Frosch, oder als ein Vogel, wie Eule oder Adler, gelegentlich als Wurm oder Käfer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Geldmännlein adalah makhluk mirip kobold dalam \"mitologi rendah\", roh rumah yang memasok uang bagi pemiliknya; tak selalu berwujud lelaki kecil, ia bisa tampil sebagai kodok, katak, burung hantu, elang, cacing, atau kumbang.",
        "en": "The Geldmännlein is a kobold-like being of 'lower mythology', a house spirit that provides its owner with money; not always a little man, it may appear as a toad, frog, owl, eagle, worm or beetle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c02",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s1",
      "quote": "Nah verwandt ist die Sage vom Drak in Gestalt eines feurigen Drachens. Ähnliche Motive finden sich im Märchen vom Goldesel und der Fabel von der Gans, die goldene Eier legt. Fließend ist der Übergang zu menschenähnlichen Figuren, die man aus Wurzeln, wie der Alraune, herstellt",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Drak dalam wujud naga api berkerabat dekat; motif serupa ada dalam dongeng keledai emas dan angsa bertelur emas, dan peralihannya ke sosok mirip manusia dari akar seperti Alraune berlangsung mulus.",
        "en": "Closely related is the legend of the Drak as a fiery dragon; similar motifs appear in the tale of the gold donkey and the goose that lays golden eggs, and it shades into human-like root figures such as the Alraune."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c03",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s1",
      "quote": "Die erste namentliche Erwähnung findet sich 1657 in den Gerichtsakten gegen einen Caspar Michel Fuchs aus Sulzfeld im Grabfeld bei Meiningen, der beschuldigt wurde, ein geldtmännlein zu besitzen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penyebutan pertama dengan nama ini terdapat pada 1657 dalam berkas pengadilan terhadap Caspar Michel Fuchs dari Sulzfeld dekat Meiningen, yang dituduh memiliki geldtmännlein.",
        "en": "The first mention by name is in 1657 court records against Caspar Michel Fuchs of Sulzfeld near Meiningen, accused of owning a geldtmännlein."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c04",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s1",
      "quote": "Das Geldmännlein befand sich, je nach Gestalt und Größe, in einer Kiste, einer Schachtel oder einem Topf, in welche man am Abend Geld legte. Am nächsten Morgen nahm man das „ausgebrütete“ Geld heraus, aber niemals alles, damit sich der Rest weiter vermehren konnte. Wie anderen Hausgeistern wurden dem Geldmännlein Speiseopfer dargebracht, wie Milch, Brot oder Brei.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Geldmännlein disimpan dalam peti, kotak, atau periuk tempat uang diletakkan pada malam hari; paginya uang yang \"dierami\" diambil, tetapi tak pernah semuanya agar sisanya terus berlipat; seperti roh rumah lain ia diberi sesaji susu, roti, atau bubur.",
        "en": "The Geldmännlein was kept in a chest, box or pot where money was put in the evening; next morning the 'hatched' money was taken out, never all, so the rest could multiply; like other house spirits it got offerings of milk, bread or porridge."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c05",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s1",
      "quote": "Aber schon Martin Luther erwähnte in seinen Tischreden eine Bäuerin, die man verdächtigte, einen Heckergroschen zu haben.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Martin Luther dalam Tischreden-nya sudah menyebut seorang petani perempuan yang dicurigai memiliki Heckergroschen (uang pelipat).",
        "en": "Martin Luther in his Tischreden already mentioned a peasant woman suspected of having a Heckergroschen (breeding coin)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c06",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s2",
      "quote": "Je také nazýván také Heckemännchen „mužíček živého plotu“, Geldschisser „peníze kálející“ či Geldbrüter „peníze rodící“.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga disebut Heckemännchen, Geldschisser, atau Geldbrüter.",
        "en": "It is also called Heckemännchen, Geldschisser or Geldbrüter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c07",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s2",
      "quote": "Má podobu šedivého mužíčka, někdy s červenou čapkou a zelenou vestou, a někdy se mění ve zvířata – žábu, ropuchu, sovu či brouka.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berwujud lelaki kecil kelabu, kadang bertopi merah dan berompi hijau, dan kadang berubah menjadi hewan seperti katak, kodok, burung hantu, atau kumbang.",
        "en": "It looks like a little grey man, sometimes with a red cap and green vest, and sometimes turns into animals such as a frog, toad, owl or beetle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c08",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s2",
      "quote": "Na rozdíl od draca jej téměř nemožné se jej zbavit, první dva majitelé jej mohou prodat, ale duše třetího propadne peklu.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbeda dengan Drac (draca), Geldmännlein hampir mustahil disingkirkan: dua pemilik pertama boleh menjualnya, tetapi jiwa pemilik ketiga jatuh ke neraka.",
        "en": "Unlike the Drac (draca), it is almost impossible to get rid of: the first two owners may sell it, but the third owner's soul falls to hell."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c09",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s2",
      "quote": "Označení se liší především krajově, Geldmännlein je typický pro Sasko a Durynsko, Geldschisser pro Bavorsko, Alraunel pro Dolní Rakousy.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutannya berbeda menurut daerah: Geldmännlein khas di Sachsen (Sasko) dan Thüringen (Durynsko), Geldschisser di Bayern (Bavorsko), Alraunel di Austria Hilir (Dolní Rakousy).",
        "en": "Names vary by region: Geldmännlein is typical of Saxony (Sasko) and Thuringia (Durynsko), Geldschisser of Bavaria (Bavorsko), Alraunel of Lower Austria (Dolní Rakousy)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c10",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s1",
      "quote": "Wenn ihm die Speisen nicht zusagten, konnte es zornig werden und sich rächen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila makanannya tak berkenan, ia bisa marah dan membalas dendam.",
        "en": "If the food did not please it, it could grow angry and take revenge."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c11",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s1",
      "quote": "Luther stand solchem Volksglauben äußerst ablehnend gegenüber",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Luther sangat menolak kepercayaan rakyat semacam itu.",
        "en": "Luther was utterly opposed to such folk belief."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geldmannlein-c12",
      "source_id": "geldmannlein-s2",
      "quote": "Geldmännlein „peněžní mužíček“ je v německém folklóru bytost či kouzelný předmět magicky přinášející svému majiteli peníze.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Geldmännlein (\"lelaki kecil uang\") dalam cerita rakyat Jerman (German; německém) adalah makhluk atau benda ajaib yang secara gaib membawa uang bagi pemiliknya.",
        "en": "In German (německém) folklore the Geldmännlein ('little money man') is a being or magic object that magically brings its owner money."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia bahasa Jerman dan Ceko; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}
```

```json
      "en": "In Norse mythology Gísl appears in Grímnismál and Gylfaginning among the horses of the Æsir, which are chiefly ridden every day to the world tree Yggdrasil so that their riders can pass judgement, crossing the bridge Bifröst on the way. No source says which god rides it.",

    {
      "id": "gisl-c01",
      "source_id": "gisl-s1",
      "quote": "Their main purpose is to be ridden daily to Yggdrasil in order for their riders to pass judgements. They are said to cross Bifröst along their journey.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuda-kuda Æsir (termasuk Gísl, yang tercantum dalam Grímnismál dan Gylfaginning) terutama ditunggangi setiap hari ke Yggdrasil agar penunggangnya dapat memberi keputusan, dan konon melintasi Bifröst dalam perjalanannya.",
        "en": "The horses of the Æsir (Gísl among them, listed in Grímnismál and Gylfaginning) are mainly ridden daily to Yggdrasil so their riders can pass judgement, and are said to cross Bifröst on the way."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gisl-c02",
      "source_id": "gisl-s2",
      "quote": "Gísl er en hest i nordisk mytologi, som bliver nævnt i både Grímnismál og Gylfaginning som en af de hingste, som aserne rider på hver dag, når drager til Yggdrasil for at dømme ting. I ingen af stanzaerne bliver ejerskabet af hesten tilskrevet til en bestemt gud, som det er tilfældet med bl.a. Sleipner og Frejfaxe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Denmark menyebut Gísl kuda dalam mitologi Nordik (nordisk) yang ditunggangi para Æsir (aserne) ke Yggdrasil, dan tak satu bait pun menyebut pemiliknya, berbeda dengan Sleipnir (Sleipner) dan Freyfaxi (Frejfaxe).",
        "en": "The Danish article calls Gísl a horse of Norse (nordisk) mythology ridden by the Æsir (aserne) to Yggdrasil, with no stanza naming its owner, unlike Sleipnir (Sleipner) and Freyfaxi (Frejfaxe)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gisl-c03",
      "source_id": "gisl-s1",
      "quote": "These are the names of the Æsir's steeds: Sleipnir is best, which Odin has; he has eight feet. The second is Gladr, the third Gyllir, the fourth Glenr, the fifth Skeidbrimir, the sixth Silfrintoppr, the seventh Sinir, the eighth Gisl, the ninth Falhófnir, the tenth Gulltoppr, the eleventh Léttfeti.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gylfaginning (terjemahan Brodeur) mendaftar kuda para Æsir: Sleipnir yang terbaik milik Odin dan berkaki delapan, lalu Gladr, Gyllir, Glenr, Skeidbrimir, Silfrintoppr, Sinir, Gisl, Falhófnir, Gulltoppr, dan Léttfeti.",
        "en": "Gylfaginning (Brodeur's translation) lists the Æsir's steeds: Sleipnir, Odin's eight-footed best, then Gladr, Gyllir, Glenr, Skeidbrimir, Silfrintoppr, Sinir, Gisl, Falhófnir, Gulltoppr and Léttfeti."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gisl-c04",
      "source_id": "gisl-s1",
      "quote": "Gísl: related to \"beam\", \"ray\";",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Gísl berkaitan dengan \"balok\" atau \"sinar\".",
        "en": "The name Gísl is related to 'beam' or 'ray'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gisl-c05",
      "source_id": "gisl-s1",
      "quote": "Apart from Odin's eight-legged horse Sleipnir, and Gulltoppr, who belongs to Heimdallr according to the Prose Edda, nothing is known about these horses, especially their owner other than that they are ridden by the Æsir. These names aren't listed in the þulur.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selain Sleipnir milik Odin dan Gulltoppr milik Heimdallr, tak ada yang diketahui tentang kuda-kuda ini, termasuk pemiliknya, selain bahwa mereka ditunggangi para Æsir; nama-nama ini tidak tercantum dalam þulur.",
        "en": "Apart from Odin's Sleipnir and Heimdallr's Gulltoppr, nothing is known of these horses, including their owners, except that the Æsir ride them; the names are absent from the þulur."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
      "id": "gl-r-c02",
      "source_id": "gl-r-s2",
      "quote": "Glær (norrønt for \"klar\" eller \"glasagtig\") er en hest i nordisk mytologi, som bliver nævnt i både Grímnismál og Gylfaginning som en af de hingste, som aserne rider på hver dag, når drager til Yggdrasil for at dømme ting. I ingen af stanzaerne bliver ejerskabet af hesten tilskrevet til en bestemt gud, som det er tilfældet med bl.a. Sleipner og Frejfaxe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Denmark mengartikan nama Glær sebagai \"bening\" atau \"seperti kaca\"; ia salah satu kuda jantan yang ditunggangi para Æsir (aserne) ke Yggdrasil, dan tak satu bait pun menyebut pemiliknya, berbeda dengan Sleipnir (Sleipner) dan Freyfaxi (Frejfaxe).",
        "en": "The Danish article glosses Glær as 'clear' or 'glassy'; it is one of the stallions the Æsir (aserne) ride to Yggdrasil, and no stanza names its owner, unlike Sleipnir (Sleipner) and Freyfaxi (Frejfaxe)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gl-r-c03",
      "source_id": "gl-r-s3",
      "quote": "These are the names of the Æsir's steeds: Sleipnir is best, which Odin has; he has eight feet. The second is Gladr, the third Gyllir, the fourth Glenr, the fifth Skeidbrimir, the sixth Silfrintoppr, the seventh Sinir, the eighth Gisl, the ninth Falhófnir, the tenth Gulltoppr, the eleventh Léttfeti.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gylfaginning (terjemahan Brodeur) mendaftar kuda para Æsir: Sleipnir yang terbaik milik Odin dan berkaki delapan, lalu Gladr, Gyllir, Glenr, Skeidbrimir, Silfrintoppr, Sinir, Gisl, Falhófnir, Gulltoppr, dan Léttfeti.",
        "en": "Gylfaginning (Brodeur's translation) lists the Æsir's steeds: Sleipnir, Odin's eight-footed best, then Gladr, Gyllir, Glenr, Skeidbrimir, Silfrintoppr, Sinir, Gisl, Falhófnir, Gulltoppr and Léttfeti."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

}
```

```json
        "id": "Gylfaginning (terjemahan Brodeur) mendaftar kuda para Æsir: Sleipnir yang terbaik milik Odin dan berkaki delapan, lalu Gladr, Gyllir, Glenr, Skeidbrimir, Silfrintoppr, Sinir, Gisl, Falhófnir, Gulltoppr, dan Léttfeti.",
        "en": "Gylfaginning (Brodeur's translation) lists the Æsir's steeds: Sleipnir, Odin's eight-footed best, then Gladr, Gyllir, Glenr, Skeidbrimir, Silfrintoppr, Sinir, Gisl, Falhófnir, Gulltoppr and Léttfeti."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gyllir-c04",
      "source_id": "gyllir-s1",
      "quote": "Gyllir is also found in the thulur as a jötunn name.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gyllir juga ditemukan dalam þulur sebagai nama jötunn.",
        "en": "Gyllir also appears in the þulur as a jötunn name."
      }
    }
```

```json
    "id": "Haymon adalah raksasa legenda dari Tirol, pendiri Biara Wilten di Innsbruck, yang menurut cerita membunuh seekor naga dan raksasa Thyrsus.",
    "en": "Haymon is a legendary giant from Tyrol, founder of Wilten Abbey in Innsbruck, who in the tales slew a dragon and the giant Thyrsus.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "haymon-c01",
      "haymon-c03",

    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Haymon adalah tokoh mitis berwujud raksasa dari Tirol. Menurut legenda ia pendiri Biara Wilten di selatan Innsbruck dan penguasa Tirol, hidup antara 600 dan 900 tahun, dan wafat pada tahun 878 di Wilten; makamnya di dekat altar gereja biara Wilten sudah disebut pada abad ke-13. Ia mungkin seorang bangsawan Bayern bernama Haimo.",
      "en": "Haymon is a mythical figure from Tyrol in the form of a giant. Legend makes him the founder of Wilten Abbey south of Innsbruck and ruler of Tyrol, living between 600 and 900 years and dying in 878 at Wilten; his grave by the altar of the Wilten collegiate church is already mentioned in the 13th century. He may have been a Bavarian nobleman named Haimo.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "haymon-c01",
        "haymon-c05",
        "haymon-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Beberapa tradisi melebur dalam legenda Haymon. Pada abad ke-15 muncul kisah naga penjaga emas di ngarai Sill yang dibunuh Haymon; lidah naga itu, sebenarnya tonjolan tanduk ikan todak berbingkai emas, masih dipamerkan di Museum Negara Tirol Ferdinandeum. Sekitar abad ke-16 muncul kisah pertarungan Haymon melawan raksasa Thyrsus dari daerah Zirl dan Seefeld, yang darahnya konon menjadi minyak batu obat Tirol.",
      "en": "Several traditions merged in the Haymon legend. In the 15th century arose the tale of the gold-guarding dragon of the Sill gorge, slain by Haymon; its tongue, actually the horn extension of a swordfish set in gold, is still shown in the Tyrolean State Museum Ferdinandeum. Around the 16th century came the story of Haymon fighting the giant Thyrsus of the Zirl and Seefeld area, whose blood was said to become the healing Tyrolean shale oil.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "haymon-c02",
        "haymon-c03",
        "haymon-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Karena menyesal, Haymon memeluk agama Kristen, dibaptis oleh Uskup Chur, mendirikan Biara Wilten, menyerahkannya kepada para biarawan Benediktin dari Tegernsee, dan menjadi bruder hingga wafat. Pada abad ke-17 tafsiran ilmiah menganggap Thyrsus melambangkan orang Retoroman dan Haymon orang Bayern yang datang menghuni lembah Inn. Patung seukuran manusia kedua raksasa itu berdiri di pintu masuk gereja Wilten.",
      "en": "Out of remorse, Haymon took up the Christian faith, was baptised by the Bishop of Chur, founded Wilten Abbey, handed it to Benedictine monks from Tegernsee and became a lay brother until his death. Seventeenth-century scholarly interpretation saw Thyrsus as standing for the Romansh people and Haymon for the Bavarians who settled the Inn valley. Life-size figures of both giants stand at the entrance to the Wilten church.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "haymon-c07",
        "haymon-c08",
        "haymon-c10"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "haymon-c01",
      "source_id": "haymon-s1",
      "quote": "Haymon is a mythical figure from Tyrol in form of a giant. It is said that Haymon was the founder of Stift Wilten [de], a premonstratensian abbey, located in the south of Innsbruck. He is said to have lived between 600 and 900 years ago and to have died in the year 878 A.D. in Wilten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Haymon adalah tokoh mitis berwujud raksasa dari Tirol (Tyrol); ia konon pendiri Stift Wilten, biara Premonstratensian di selatan Innsbruck, hidup antara 600 dan 900 tahun, dan wafat tahun 878 M di Wilten.",
        "en": "Haymon is a mythical giant figure from Tyrol (Austria); he is said to have founded Stift Wilten, a Premonstratensian abbey south of Innsbruck, to have lived between 600 and 900 years ago, and died in 878 AD in Wilten."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c02",
      "source_id": "haymon-s1",
      "quote": "Different traditions have been amalgamated in the legend of the giant Haymon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbagai tradisi melebur dalam legenda raksasa Haymon.",
        "en": "Various traditions have merged in the legend of the giant Haymon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c03",
      "source_id": "haymon-s2",
      "quote": "Im 15. Jahrhundert entstand die lokale Sage von dem goldhütenden Drachen in der Sillschlucht östlich des Bergisel, der von Haymon erschlagen wird. Der Drache könnte für den Fluss Sill stehen, die damals häufig Hochwasser führte. Die Drachenzunge (der Hornfortsatz eines Schwertfisches) wird noch heute, in Gold gefasst, im Tiroler Landesmuseum Ferdinandeum in Innsbruck gezeigt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada abad ke-15 muncul legenda lokal tentang naga penjaga emas di ngarai Sill timur Bergisel yang dibunuh Haymon; naga itu mungkin melambangkan Sungai Sill yang sering banjir; lidah naga (tonjolan tanduk ikan todak) berbingkai emas masih dipamerkan di Museum Negara Tirol (Tiroler) Ferdinandeum di Innsbruck.",
        "en": "In the 15th century the local legend arose of the gold-guarding dragon in the Sill gorge east of Bergisel, killed by Haymon; the dragon may stand for the often-flooding Sill river; the dragon's tongue (a swordfish's horn extension), set in gold, is still shown in the Tyrolean (Tiroler) State Museum Ferdinandeum in Innsbruck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c04",
      "source_id": "haymon-s2",
      "quote": "Ein zweiter Überlieferungsstrang, der etwa im 16. Jahrhundert aufkommt, lässt Haymon mit dem Riesen Thyrsus, der in der Gegend von Zirl und Seefeld lebte, kämpfen und ihn erschlagen. Aus dessen Thyrsenblut wird das heilkräftige Tiroler Steinöl bzw. Ichthyol gewonnen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi kedua yang muncul sekitar abad ke-16 menceritakan Haymon bertarung dengan raksasa Thyrsus dari daerah Zirl dan Seefeld dan membunuhnya; dari darah Thyrsus konon diperoleh minyak batu (Steinöl) Tirol yang berkhasiat obat.",
        "en": "A second strand from around the 16th century has Haymon fight and kill the giant Thyrsus of the Zirl and Seefeld area; from Thyrsus's blood the healing Tyrolean shale oil (Steinöl) is said to come."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c05",
      "source_id": "haymon-s2",
      "quote": "Der Riese Haymon ist eine Sagenfigur aus Tirol. Der Sage nach ist er der Gründer des Klosters Wilten im Süden Innsbrucks und war Landesherr in Tirol.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raksasa (Riese) Haymon adalah tokoh legenda dari Tirol; menurut legenda ia pendiri Biara Wilten di selatan Innsbruck dan penguasa Tirol.",
        "en": "The giant (Riese) Haymon is a legendary figure from Tyrol; legend makes him founder of Wilten monastery south of Innsbruck and ruler of Tyrol."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c06",
      "source_id": "haymon-s2",
      "quote": "Haymons Grab beim Altar der Wiltener Stiftskirche wird bereits im 13. Jahrhundert erwähnt. Wahrscheinlich war er ein bayerischer Adeliger namens Haimo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makam Haymon di dekat altar gereja biara Wilten sudah disebut pada abad ke-13; kemungkinan ia bangsawan Bayern (bayerischer) bernama Haimo.",
        "en": "Haymon's grave by the altar of the Wilten collegiate church is mentioned as early as the 13th century; he was probably a Bavarian (bayerischer) nobleman named Haimo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c07",
      "source_id": "haymon-s1",
      "quote": "In remorse, Haymon takes the Christian faith being baptized by the Bishop of Chur. Thereafter, he founded the monastery Wilten which he hands over to Benedictine monks from the Tegernsee Abbey. Then he joined the order as a lay brother and stayed there until his death.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena menyesal, Haymon memeluk agama Kristen (Christian), dibaptis oleh Uskup Chur, mendirikan Biara Wilten dan menyerahkannya kepada biarawan Benediktin dari Biara Tegernsee, lalu menjadi bruder hingga wafat.",
        "en": "In remorse Haymon became a Christian, baptised by the Bishop of Chur, founded Wilten monastery and gave it to Benedictine monks from Tegernsee Abbey, then joined the order as a lay brother until death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c08",
      "source_id": "haymon-s1",
      "quote": "According to contemporary scholarly interpretation of the 17th Century, the giant Thyrsus represents the Romansh people while the invading Bavarians, who populated the Inn valley as of the 6th century, are being represented through Haymon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut tafsiran ilmiah abad ke-17, raksasa Thyrsus melambangkan orang Retoroman (Romansh), sedangkan Haymon melambangkan orang Bayern (Bavarians) pendatang yang menghuni lembah Inn sejak abad ke-6.",
        "en": "Seventeenth-century scholarly interpretation saw Thyrsus as the Romansh people and Haymon as the incoming Bavarians who settled the Inn valley from the 6th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c09",
      "source_id": "haymon-s1",
      "quote": "The book publisher Haymon Verlag is named after the mythical figure.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penerbit buku Haymon Verlag dinamai menurut tokoh mitis ini.",
        "en": "The publisher Haymon Verlag is named after the mythical figure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haymon-c10",
      "source_id": "haymon-s1",
      "quote": "Life-size figures of the two giants Haymon and Thyrsus can be found at Wilten where they are at the entrance to the church.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patung seukuran manusia kedua raksasa, Haymon dan Thyrsus, dapat ditemukan di pintu masuk gereja Wilten.",
        "en": "Life-size figures of the two giants Haymon and Thyrsus stand at the entrance to the church at Wilten."
      }
    }
```

```json
    "id": "Himinhrjóðr adalah lembu jantan terbesar milik raksasa Hymir dalam mitologi Nordik, yang kepalanya dipakai Thor sebagai umpan untuk memancing Ular Midgard.",
    "en": "Himinhrjóðr is the greatest ox of the giant Hymir in Norse mythology, whose head Thor used as bait to fish for the Midgard Serpent.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "himinhrjod-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Himinhrjóðr adalah lembu jantan dalam mitologi Nordik, sapi pejantan terbesar dan terindah milik raksasa Hymir. Menurut Snorri Sturluson dalam Gylfaginning, ketika Thor pergi memancing bersama Hymir dan Hymir menyuruhnya mencari umpan sendiri, Thor mengambil lembu terbesar dari kawanan Hymir, mencabut kepalanya, dan membawanya ke pantai.",
      "en": "Himinhrjóðr is an ox of Norse mythology, the largest and finest breeding bull of the giant Hymir. According to Snorri Sturluson's Gylfaginning, when Thor went fishing with Hymir and Hymir told him to find his own bait, Thor took the biggest ox of Hymir's herd, wrenched off its head and carried it to the shore.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "himinhrjod-c01",
        "himinhrjod-c02",
        "himinhrjod-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada kepala lembu itulah Ular Midgard kemudian menggigit kail; namun Hymir begitu ketakutan melihat tangkapan mereka sehingga ia memotong tali pancing, dan ular itu tenggelam kembali ke dasar laut. Kisah memancing Thor juga disebut dalam Hymiskviða dan karya para skald lain, tetapi hanya Snorri yang mencatat nama lembu Hymir.",
      "en": "It was on this ox head that the Midgard Serpent later took the hook; but Hymir was so terrified at the catch that he cut the line, and the serpent sank back into the depths of the sea. Thor's fishing trip is also told in Hymiskviða and by other skalds, but only Snorri records the name of Hymir's ox.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "himinhrjod-c04",
        "himinhrjod-c05",
        "himinhrjod-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Himinhrjóðr mungkin berarti \"perusak langit\" bila bagian akhirnya dari kata kerja hrjóða; Finnur Jónsson membayangkan tanduknya begitu tinggi sehingga tampak menembus langit. Varian lain menghasilkan arti seperti \"pewarna merah langit\" atau \"pemecah langit\". Rudolf Simek menduga Snorri sendiri yang memberi nama itu, tetapi nama tersebut juga muncul dalam daftar þulur nama lembu.",
      "en": "The name Himinhrjóðr may mean 'sky-destroyer' if its second part comes from the verb hrjóða; Finnur Jónsson imagined its horns so high that they seemed to pierce the sky. Other variants yield meanings such as 'reddener of the sky' or 'sky-breaker'. Rudolf Simek guesses that Snorri himself coined the name, but it also occurs in the þulur lists of ox names.",

        "himinhrjod-c06",
        "himinhrjod-c08",
        "himinhrjod-c09"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "url": "https://sv.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himinhrj%C3%B3%C3%B0r",
      "title": "Himinhrjóðr",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (sv)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "sv",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c01",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s1",
      "quote": "Himinhrjod er i nordisk mytologi navnet på jætten Hymers største og flotteste avlstyr.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik (nordisk), Himinhrjod adalah nama sapi pejantan terbesar dan terindah milik raksasa Hymir (Hymers).",
        "en": "In Norse (nordisk) mythology Himinhrjod is the name of the giant Hymir's (Hymers) biggest and finest breeding bull."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c02",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "quote": "Himinhrjóðr (med flera stavningsvarianter) är en oxe i nordisk mytologi. Enligt Snorre Sturlasson var det denna oxes huvud som Tor satte på kroken när han var ute på fiske med jätten Hymer och råkade fånga Midgårdsormen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Himinhrjóðr adalah lembu dalam mitologi Nordik; menurut Snorri Sturluson (Snorre Sturlasson), kepala lembu inilah yang dipasang Thor (Tor) pada kail saat memancing bersama raksasa Hymir (Hymer) dan menangkap Ular Midgard (Midgårdsormen).",
        "en": "Himinhrjóðr is an ox of Norse mythology; according to Snorri Sturluson (Snorre Sturlasson) it was this ox's head Thor (Tor) put on the hook when fishing with the giant Hymir (Hymer) and catching the Midgard Serpent (Midgårdsormen)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c03",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "quote": "Han [Tor] frågade Hymer vad de skulle ha till bete, men Hymer sade åt honom att skaffa sig bete själv. Då gav sig Tor åstad tills han såg en flock oxar som Hymer ägde. Han tog den största oxen, som hette Himinhrjótr, slet av den huvudet och gick med det till stranden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Thor (Tor) bertanya kepada Hymir (Hymer) apa umpannya, dan Hymir menyuruhnya mencari sendiri; Thor menemukan kawanan lembu Hymir, mengambil yang terbesar bernama Himinhrjótr, mencabut kepalanya, dan membawanya ke pantai (Gylfaginning).",
        "en": "Thor (Tor) asked Hymir (Hymer) what bait to use, and Hymir told him to get his own; Thor found Hymir's herd of oxen, took the largest, named Himinhrjótr, tore off its head and went to the shore (Gylfaginning)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c04",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "quote": "Det var på detta oxhuvud som Midgårdsormen senare nappade, men när Hymer såg vilken fångst de fått blev han så förskräckt att han högg av reven, varpå ormen sjönk tillbaka i havets djup.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada kepala lembu inilah Ular Midgard (Midgårdsormen) kemudian menggigit, tetapi Hymir (Hymer) begitu ketakutan melihat tangkapan itu sehingga memotong tali pancing, dan ular itu tenggelam kembali ke dasar laut.",
        "en": "It was this ox head the Midgard Serpent (Midgårdsormen) later bit, but Hymir (Hymer) was so terrified at the catch that he cut the line, and the serpent sank back into the depths."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c05",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "quote": "Men i ingen av dessa källor nämns namnet på Hymers oxe – Snorre är ensam om den uppgiften.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tak satu pun sumber lain menyebut nama lembu Hymir (Hymers); hanya Snorri (Snorre) yang mencatatnya.",
        "en": "None of the other sources names Hymir's (Hymers) ox; only Snorri (Snorre) does."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c06",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "quote": "Himinhrjóðr kan betyda ”himmelsförstörare” om sista ledet kommer av verbet hrjóða, ”röja, göra rent hus, länsa”. Finnur Jónsson tänkte sig hornen vara så höga ”at de synes at gennembore himlen”.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Himinhrjóðr bisa berarti \"perusak langit\" bila bagian akhirnya dari kata kerja hrjóða; Finnur Jónsson membayangkan tanduknya begitu tinggi sehingga tampak menembus langit.",
        "en": "Himinhrjóðr may mean 'sky-destroyer' if the last element comes from the verb hrjóða; Finnur Jónsson pictured its horns so high they seemed to pierce the sky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c07",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "quote": "Snorres källa tycks ha varit Hymiskviða, stroferna 18–25. Men Tors fiske omtalas också i Brage den gamles Ragnarsdrápa och i Húsdrápa av Úlfr Uggason.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber Snorri (Snorres) tampaknya Hymiskviða, bait 18–25; kisah memancing Thor (Tors) juga disebut dalam Ragnarsdrápa karya Bragi (Brage) Tua dan Húsdrápa karya Úlfr Uggason.",
        "en": "Snorri's (Snorres) source seems to have been Hymiskviða, stanzas 18–25; Thor's (Tors) fishing is also told in Bragi's (Brage) Ragnarsdrápa and Úlfr Uggason's Húsdrápa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c08",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "quote": "Rudolf Simek gissar att det var Snorre själv som lät Hymers oxe heta Himinhrjóðr. Men namnet finns även i tulornas Øxna heiti, liksom i Torgrimstulans andra fragment.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rudolf Simek menduga Snorri (Snorre) sendiri yang menamai lembu Hymir (Hymers) Himinhrjóðr, tetapi nama itu juga ada dalam Øxna heiti di tula (þulur).",
        "en": "Rudolf Simek guesses Snorri (Snorre) himself named Hymir's (Hymers) ox Himinhrjóðr, but the name also appears in the Øxna heiti of the þulur."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "himinhrjod-c09",
      "source_id": "himinhrjod-s2",
      "quote": "Uppsala-Eddan har stavningen Himinrjóðr, vilket betyder ”himlens rödfärgare”, av rjóða, ”färga röd”. Slutligen kan namnet vara Himinbrjótr av brjóta, ”bryta, slå sönder, förstöra”.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Edda Uppsala mengeja Himinrjóðr, \"pewarna merah langit\", dari rjóða; nama itu juga mungkin Himinbrjótr, dari brjóta, \"mematahkan, menghancurkan\".",
        "en": "The Uppsala Edda spells it Himinrjóðr, 'reddener of the sky', from rjóða; the name might also be Himinbrjótr, from brjóta, 'break, smash'."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kludde adalah setan pengubah wujud dalam cerita rakyat Flandria dan Belanda selatan, yang dengan rantai berdenting melompat ke tengkuk pejalan malam dan harus digendong hingga fajar.",
    "en": "The Kludde is a shapeshifting demon of Flemish and southern Dutch folklore who, with a rattling chain, leaps onto night walkers' necks and must be carried until dawn.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kludde-c01",
      "kludde-c02",
      "kludde-c04",
      "kludde-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kludde adalah makhluk pengubah wujud dalam kepercayaan Jermanik dan Flandria, dikenal di Belanda serta Flandria Belgia dan Prancis. Rupanya berbeda dari cerita ke cerita: anjing hitam mengerikan yang berjalan dengan kaki belakang, kucing hitam besar, burung hitam raksasa, atau kuda; ia dapat membesar dan mengecil, dan makin cepat pejalan lari, makin cepat ia mengejar. Dalam cerita lain ia lelaki berbalut kulit anjing berbulu, mengingatkan pada manusia serigala.",
      "en": "The Kludde is a shapeshifter of Germanic and Flemish belief, known in the Netherlands and in Belgian and French Flanders. Its looks vary from tale to tale: a monstrous black dog walking on its hind legs, a big black cat, a giant black bird or a horse; it can grow and shrink, and the faster passers-by run, the faster it follows. In other tales it is a man wrapped in a shaggy dog skin, recalling the werewolf.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kludde-c01",
        "kludde-c02",
        "kludde-c03",
        "kludde-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kludde adalah roh pengganggu yang bersembunyi di bawah jembatan atau di pohon berongga dan hanya keluar pada malam hari. Pejalan tahu ia datang dari bunyi rantai yang wajib dipakainya di pergelangan kaki kiri. Ia lalu melompat ke tengkuk orang yang lengah, yang harus menggendongnya sepanjang malam sampai fajar atau sampai tiba di tujuan. Dalam wujud kuda ia memperdaya para pengurus kandang dan melempar mereka ke parit.",
      "en": "The Kludde is a tormenting spirit that hides under bridges or in hollow trees and comes out only at night. Walkers know it is coming from the sound of the chain it must wear on its left ankle. It then leaps onto an unsuspecting walker's neck, and the victim must carry it all night until daybreak or until reaching home. In horse form it tricks stable boys and throws them into a ditch.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kludde-c04",
        "kludde-c05",
        "kludde-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kludde dianggap tak terkalahkan, tetapi beberapa kisah menceritakan kekalahannya: di Schelle seorang tukang daging menikam perutnya hingga ia lari dan tak kembali, dan cerita lain menyebut doa sebagai cara meloloskan diri. Ia dikaitkan dengan daerah Schelde, Dendermonde, sekitar Antwerpen, serta Brabant Flandria; di Aalst ia disebut Kledden.",
      "en": "The Kludde was thought unbeatable, yet a few tales tell of its defeat: in Schelle a butcher stabbed it in the belly so that it fled and never returned, and other stories name prayer as a way to escape. It is associated with the Scheldt region, Dendermonde, the Antwerp area and Flemish Brabant; in Aalst it was called Kledden.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kludde-c06",
        "kludde-c07",
        "kludde-c08",
        "kludde-c11",
        "kludde-c12"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [

      "source_id": "kludde-s2",
      "quote": "Kludde est une créature métamorphe des croyances germaniques et flamandes, mentionnée aux Pays-Bas et en Flandre, tant belge que française. C'est un métamorphe, qui prend de nombreuses formes dont celle d'un chien noir, d'un cheval et d'un chat. Sous forme de cheval, il trompe les garçons d'écurie et les jette dans un fossé",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kludde adalah makhluk pengubah wujud dalam kepercayaan Jermanik dan Flandria (flamandes), disebut di Belanda (Pays-Bas) dan Flandria (Flandre) Belgia maupun Prancis; ia mengambil banyak wujud seperti anjing hitam, kuda, dan kucing, dan dalam wujud kuda memperdaya pengurus kandang lalu melempar mereka ke parit.",
        "en": "Kludde is a shapeshifting creature of Germanic and Flemish belief, known in the Netherlands and in Belgian and French Flanders; it takes many forms, a black dog, a horse, a cat, and as a horse tricks stable boys and throws them into a ditch."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c02",

      "quote": "Kludde is een fictieve figuur uit de Vlaamse en Zuid-Nederlandse folklore.",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Kludde adalah tokoh dari cerita rakyat Flandria (Vlaamse) dan Belanda selatan (Zuid-Nederlandse).",
        "en": "Kludde is a figure of Flemish (Vlaamse) and southern Dutch (Zuid-Nederlandse) folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c03",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "In sommige vertellingen wordt hij omschreven als een monsterlijke zwarte hond die zich op zijn achterste poten voorbeweegt.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam beberapa cerita ia anjing hitam mengerikan yang berjalan dengan kaki belakang; ia juga bisa menjadi kucing hitam besar atau burung hitam raksasa; makin cepat pejalan lari, makin cepat ia mengejar, dan ia dapat membesar serta mengecil.",
        "en": "In some tales it is a monstrous black dog walking on its hind legs; it can also become a big black cat or giant black bird; the faster passers-by flee, the faster it follows, and it can grow and shrink."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c04",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "Kludde is een kwelgeest die zich vaak onder bruggen of in holle bomen schuilhoudt en enkel 's nachts tevoorschijn komt. Wandelaars weten uitsluitend aan de hand van het geluid dat hij maakt, dat hij eraan komt. Kludde rammelt namelijk met een ketting die hij verplicht is aan zijn linkerenkel te dragen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kludde adalah roh pengganggu yang bersembunyi di bawah jembatan atau di pohon berongga dan hanya keluar malam hari; pejalan hanya tahu kedatangannya dari bunyi rantai yang wajib dipakainya di pergelangan kaki kiri.",
        "en": "Kludde is a tormenting spirit hiding under bridges or in hollow trees, emerging only at night; walkers know it comes only from the rattle of the chain it must wear on its left ankle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c05",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "Als hij eenmaal zijn komst heeft laten merken, springt hij de nietsvermoedende voorbijganger in zijn of haar nek. Deze is dan verplicht hem de rest van de nacht op zijn of haar rug te dragen. Als de dag weer aanbreekt of de voorbijganger zijn bestemming bereikt verdwijnt Kludde weer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah menandai kedatangannya, ia melompat ke tengkuk pejalan yang lengah, yang harus menggendongnya sisa malam; saat fajar tiba atau pejalan mencapai tujuan, Kludde lenyap.",
        "en": "Having announced itself, it leaps onto the unsuspecting walker's neck, who must carry it for the rest of the night; at daybreak or when the walker arrives, Kludde vanishes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c06",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "Toen hij hem opende bleek Kludde in het deurgat te staan. Onbevreesd stak de slager een mes in Kluddes buik, waarna de demon jammerend op de vlucht sloeg en nooit meer naar het huis terugkeerde.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Schelle, ketika tukang daging membuka pintu dan Kludde berdiri di ambang, ia tanpa takut menikamkan pisau ke perut Kludde, lalu setan itu lari meraung dan tak pernah kembali.",
        "en": "In Schelle, when the butcher opened the door and found Kludde in the doorway, he fearlessly stabbed it in the belly, and the demon fled wailing, never to return."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c07",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "Andere verhalen duiden bidden als een manier aan om aan het wezen te ontsnappen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita lain menyebut berdoa sebagai cara meloloskan diri dari makhluk itu.",
        "en": "Other tales name praying as a way to escape the creature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c08",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "Kludde wordt geassocieerd met de Scheldestreek; Dendermonde en andere dorpen in Oost-Vlaanderen, maar ook in de omgeving van Antwerpen, zoals Hemiksem en Schelle bestaan er verhalen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kludde dikaitkan dengan daerah Schelde (Scheldestreek), Dendermonde dan desa-desa lain di Flandria Timur, juga sekitar Antwerpen seperti Hemiksem dan Schelle.",
        "en": "Kludde is linked with the Scheldt region (Scheldestreek), Dendermonde and other East Flanders villages, and around Antwerp such as Hemiksem and Schelle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c09",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "In andere verhalen is hij eerder een man die zich in een harige hondenhuid hult, waarmee hij doet denken aan de legende van de weerwolf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita lain (andere verhalen) ia lebih merupakan lelaki (man) yang membalut diri dengan kulit anjing berbulu (harige hondenhuid), mengingatkan pada legenda manusia serigala (weerwolf).",
        "en": "In other tales (andere verhalen) it is rather a man wrapped in a hairy dog skin (harige hondenhuid), recalling the legend of the werewolf (weerwolf)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c10",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "In de strip Suske en Wiske stelde Vandersteen hem voor als een bebaard mannetje met een grote vissestaart.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam komik Suske en Wiske, Vandersteen menggambarkannya sebagai lelaki kecil berjanggut dengan ekor ikan besar.",
        "en": "In the comic Suske en Wiske, Vandersteen drew him as a bearded little man with a big fish tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c11",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "In Aalst werd hij Kledden genoemd. Hij woonde in de Dender, de Aalstenaars wisten hem te verjagen richting Dendermonde.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Aalst ia disebut Kledden dan tinggal di Sungai Dender, sampai warga Aalst mengusirnya ke arah Dendermonde.",
        "en": "In Aalst it was called Kledden and lived in the Dender, until the people of Aalst drove it towards Dendermonde."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kludde-c12",
      "source_id": "kludde-s1",
      "quote": "Eveneens is Kludde gekend in de streken rond Dilbeek, Zemst en Zemst-Laar in Vlaams Brabant, daar huist hij in de vroegere moerassen, bossen, weilanden en de Zenne. Kludde draagt daar de naam Kleudde",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kludde juga dikenal di sekitar Dilbeek, Zemst, dan Zemst-Laar di Brabant Flandria (Vlaams Brabant), tempat ia tinggal di bekas rawa, hutan, padang rumput, dan Sungai Zenne dengan nama Kleudde.",
        "en": "Kludde is also known around Dilbeek, Zemst and Zemst-Laar in Flemish Brabant (Vlaams Brabant), dwelling in former marshes, woods, meadows and the Zenne under the name Kleudde."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

    }
  ]
}
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Marmennill adalah manusia laut dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia dan Islandia yang sering tertangkap pemancing secara tak sengaja dan dikenal dapat meramalkan masa depan.",
    "en": "The marmennill is a merman of Scandinavian and Icelandic folklore, often caught by fishermen by accident and known for foretelling the future.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, marmennill (juga marmandill, marbendill, atau margmelli) adalah manusia laut yang sering muncul dalam kisah setelah tak sengaja tertangkap nelayan di laut. Ia dikenal mampu melihat masa depan atau mengungkap pengetahuan tersembunyi, dan sering tertawa bila melihat orang bertindak bodoh. Dalam kepercayaan Islandia ia bertubuh atas manusia dan berekor ikan seperti putri duyung, dan padanan perempuannya disebut margýgur.",
      "en": "In Scandinavian folklore the marmennill (also marmandill, marbendill or margmelli) is a merman who often turns up in tales after being accidentally caught at sea by fishermen. He is known for seeing the future or revealing hidden knowledge, and often laughs when he sees people acting foolishly. In Icelandic belief he has a human upper body and a fish's tail like a mermaid, and his female counterpart is called margýgur.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "marmennill-c01",
        "marmennill-c02",
        "marmennill-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Landnámabók menceritakan Grímr, yang saat memancing di Islandia menarik naik seekor marmennill dan bertanya tentang nasib mereka dan tempat menetap. Makhluk itu menjawab bahwa ia tak perlu meramalkan nasib Grímr, melainkan nasib anak yang terbaring dalam kantong kulit anjing laut: anak itu akan menetap di tempat kuda betina Skálm rebah di bawah muatannya. Musim dingin itu Grímr dan seluruh awaknya hilang di laut, sedangkan anak itu berada di darat. Marmennill peramal lain muncul dalam Hálfs saga ok Hálfsrekka abad ke-14, yang baru bernubuat dalam bentuk syair setelah dikembalikan ke laut.",
      "en": "Landnámabók tells of Grímr, who, fishing off Iceland, hauled up a marmennill and asked about their fate and where to settle. The creature answered that he need not foretell Grímr's fate, but that of the boy lying in a sealskin bag: the boy would settle where the mare Skálm lay down under her load. That winter Grímr and his whole crew were lost at sea, while the boy was ashore.       "claim_ids": [

    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah rakyat Islandia yang terkenal, \"Þá hló marbendill\", seorang petani membawa pulang marbendill yang tertawa tiga kali: karena istrinya tidak setia, karena anjing yang dipukulnya sebenarnya setia, dan karena gundukan rumput yang ia tendang menyembunyikan harta.       "en": "In the well-known Icelandic tale 'Þá hló marbendill', a farmer takes home a marbendill that laughs three times: because his wife is unfaithful, because the dog he struck truly loves him, and because the tuft of grass he kicked hides a treasure.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c01",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s1",
      "quote": "is a merman that often features in stories after having been accidentally caught at sea by fishermen. The creature is typically known for its ability to see the future or to reveal otherwise hidden knowledge, often laughing when he sees others acting foolishly.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia (Scandinavian), marmennill adalah manusia laut yang sering muncul dalam kisah setelah tak sengaja tertangkap nelayan; ia dikenal dapat melihat masa depan atau mengungkap pengetahuan tersembunyi dan sering tertawa melihat orang bertindak bodoh.",
        "en": "In Scandinavian folklore the marmennill is a merman often featured after being accidentally caught by fishermen; he is known for seeing the future or revealing hidden knowledge, and often laughs at others' folly."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c02",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s1",
      "quote": "Marmennill has been variously translated as \"merman\", \"mereman\", \"sea-mannikin\" and \"sea-goblin\". In the Hauksbók version of Landnámabók, it is spelt as margmelli , with other medieval variants including marmandill . The modern Icelandic form is marbendill .",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Marmennill diterjemahkan \"manusia laut\", \"manusia kecil laut\", atau \"goblin laut\"; dalam Landnámabók versi Hauksbók dieja margmelli, varian abad pertengahan lain marmandill, dan bentuk Islandia (Icelandic) modernnya marbendill.",
        "en": "Marmennill is rendered 'merman', 'sea-mannikin' or 'sea-goblin'; Hauksbók's Landnámabók spells it margmelli, another medieval variant is marmandill, and the modern Icelandic form is marbendill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c03",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s1",
      "quote": "Grim drew up a mereman and when he came up asked him \"What do you foretell shall be our fortune, or where shall we settle in Iceland ? The mereman answered, \"No need to tell the fortune of you and your men, but rather of the lad that lays in the seal bag, he shall there settle and take land where Skalm your mare lays down under her load,\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Landnámabók, Grímr (Grim) menarik naik manusia laut dan bertanya nasib mereka dan di mana menetap di Islandia (Iceland); jawabnya, tak perlu meramal nasib Grímr, melainkan anak dalam kantong kulit anjing laut, yang akan menetap di tempat kuda betina Skálm (Skalm) rebah di bawah muatannya.",
        "en": "In Landnámabók Grímr (Grim) hauled up a merman and asked their fortune and where to settle in Iceland; it said there was no need to foretell Grímr's fortune, but the boy's in the sealskin bag, who would settle where the mare Skálm (Skalm) lay down under her load."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c04",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s1",
      "quote": "Later in the winter Grim and his men rowed out, but the lad was upon land; then the whole crew was lost. Another prophetic marmennill appears in the 14th-century Hálfs saga ok Hálfsrekka.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kemudian di musim dingin Grímr (Grim) dan anak buahnya melaut sementara anak itu di darat, lalu seluruh awak hilang; marmennill peramal lain muncul dalam Hálfs saga ok Hálfsrekka abad ke-14.",
        "en": "Later that winter Grímr (Grim) and his men rowed out while the boy was ashore, and the whole crew was lost; another prophetic marmennill appears in the 14th-century Hálfs saga ok Hálfsrekka."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c05",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s1",
      "quote": "It tells the story of a farmer who catches a marbendill out at sea and takes it home with him. On the way home, the creature laughs three times at the farmer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah \"Þá hló marbendill\" menceritakan petani yang menangkap marbendill di laut dan membawanya pulang; dalam perjalanan makhluk itu menertawakannya tiga kali.",
        "en": "The tale 'Þá hló marbendill' tells of a farmer who catches a marbendill at sea and takes it home; on the way it laughs at him three times."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c06",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s2",
      "quote": "Marmennill är i isländsk folktro ett havsväsen som liksom sjöjungfrun har en människas överkropp och en fiskstjärt som underkropp. Varelsen menades ha förmågan att förutspå framtiden. Den kvinnliga motsvarigheten kallades Margygur och en motsvarande varelse i Sverige och Danmark kallades Havmannen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Islandia (isländsk), marmennill adalah makhluk laut yang, seperti putri duyung, bertubuh atas manusia dan berekor ikan; ia dianggap mampu meramal masa depan; padanan perempuannya Margygur, dan makhluk padanannya di Swedia (Sverige) dan Denmark (Danmark) disebut Havmannen.",
        "en": "In Icelandic (isländsk) folk belief the marmennill is a sea being which, like a mermaid, has a human upper body and a fish tail; it was thought able to foretell the future; its female counterpart was Margygur, and the corresponding being in Sweden (Sverige) and Denmark (Danmark) was Havmannen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c07",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s1",
      "quote": "The phrase þá hló marbendill (\"then the merman laughed\") is used in Iceland to refer to a sudden and spiteful fit of laughter in reference to the motif in folktales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ungkapan þá hló marbendill (\"lalu manusia laut itu tertawa\") dipakai di Islandia (Iceland) untuk tawa mendadak yang dengki, merujuk motif dalam kisah rakyat.",
        "en": "The phrase þá hló marbendill ('then the merman laughed') is used in Iceland for a sudden spiteful laugh, after the folktale motif."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c08",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s1",
      "quote": "In exchange for his freedom, the creature reveals that he laughed at the farmer because his wife is unfaithful, his dog truly loves him, and the tuft of grass conceals a treasure.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai tebusan kebebasannya, makhluk itu mengungkap bahwa ia tertawa karena istri petani tak setia, anjingnya sungguh menyayanginya, dan gundukan rumput itu menyembunyikan harta.",
        "en": "In exchange for freedom the creature reveals he laughed because the farmer's wife is unfaithful, his dog truly loves him, and the tuft of grass hides a treasure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marmennill-c09",
      "source_id": "marmennill-s1",
      "quote": "Upon being returned to the sea, however, he issues several prophecies in poetic verse.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
```

```json
    "id": "Melanchaetes (\"berambut hitam\") adalah salah satu anjing betina pemburu Aktaion dalam mitologi Yunani, yang pertama menyerang tuannya setelah Aktaion diubah menjadi rusa oleh Artemis.",
    "en": "Melanchaetes ('black-haired') is one of the hunting hounds of Actaeon in Greek mythology, the first to attack her master after Artemis turned him into a stag.",

    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Yunani, Melanchaetes (bahasa Yunani Kuno Μελαγχαίτης, \"berambut hitam\") adalah salah satu anjing betina milik pemburu Aktaion. Seperti seluruh kawanannya, ia ikut mencabik tuannya ketika Aktaion diubah menjadi rusa jantan oleh Artemis, dewi perburuan. Kisah ini terdapat dalam Metamorphoses karya Ovidius dan Fabulae karya Hyginus.",
      "en": "In Greek mythology Melanchaetes (Ancient Greek Μελαγχαίτης, 'black-haired') is one of the hounds of the hunter Actaeon. Like the rest of the pack, she helped tear her master apart when Artemis, goddess of the hunt, turned Actaeon into a stag. The story appears in Ovid's Metamorphoses and Hyginus's Fabulae.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "melanchaetes-c01",
        "melanchaetes-c02"

    {
      "id": "Menurut Ovidius, Melanchaetes tertinggal dari kawanan saat berburu, tetapi memotong jalan melewati punggung bukit, sehingga menjadi anjing pertama yang menyerang Aktaion dan melukai punggungnya, sementara dua anjing betina lain, Theridamas dan Oresitrophos, menerkam bahunya. Nama yang sama juga dipakai untuk salah satu kentaur yang mencoba mencuri anggur Pholos dan dibunuh Herakles.",
      "en": "According to Ovid, Melanchaetes fell behind the pack during the hunt but took a shortcut over a ridge, so she was the first hound to attack Actaeon and wound his back, while two other bitches, Theridamas and Oresitrophos, seized his shoulders.   "short_description": {
```
```json
    "id": "Roggenwolf (\"serigala gandum hitam\") adalah setan gandum berwujud serigala dalam legenda Jerman, roh angin di ladang yang ditakuti sebagai pemangsa anak-anak.",
    "en": "The Roggenwolf ('rye wolf') is a wolf-shaped corn demon of German legend, a wind spirit of the fields feared as a devourer of children.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "roggenwolf-c01",
      "roggenwolf-c02",
      "roggenwolf-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Roggenwolf adalah setan gandum berwujud serigala dan momok anak-anak dalam legenda Jerman, yang tinggal di ladang. Peribahasa abad ke-19 menggambarkannya rakus tak terpuaskan hingga tak bisa bergerak karena kekenyangan, serta agresif; ia melolong dan meraung karena lapar.",
      "en": "The Roggenwolf is a wolf-shaped corn demon and children's bogey of German legend that dwells in the fields. Nineteenth-century proverbs paint it as insatiably greedy, gorging itself until it cannot move, and aggressive; it howls and roars in its hunger.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "roggenwolf-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia juga roh angin, sehingga disebut Windwolf: bila angin menggerakkan bulir gandum bergelombang, orang berkata serigala sedang melintasi ladang. Ia dianggap menyuburkan atau sebaliknya merugikan ladang, karena memakan gandumnya, dan konon menyebabkan penyakit ergot pada gandum yang karena itu disebut Wolf atau Wolfszahn. Ia bersembunyi di berkas panen terakhir, yang juga disebut Wolf, dan siapa pun yang bersentuhan dengannya saat panen jatuh sakit.",
      "en": "It is also a wind spirit, hence the name Windwolf: when the wind sets the ears of grain waving, people said the wolf was going through the field. It was thought either to promote or to reduce the field's fertility, since it feeds on the grain, and is said to cause ergot, which is therefore called Wolf or Wolfszahn. It sits in the last sheaf of the harvest, also called Wolf, and anyone who touches it at harvest falls ill.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "roggenwolf-c02",
        "roggenwolf-c03",
        "roggenwolf-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagai momok anak-anak, ia menunggu anak-anak yang mencari bunga jagung atau memetik bulir gandum untuk mencabik, menggigit, memakan, atau membawa mereka pergi; jiwa anak yang dimakannya harus melayang di pepohonan sampai panen selesai. Para Roggenwolf dianggap anak Roggenmuhme. Wilhelm Mannhardt membandingkannya dengan serigala Nordik Fenrir, karena keduanya diikat, kadang dengan besi.",
      "en": "As a children's bogey it lies in wait for children looking for cornflowers or picking ears of grain, to tear, bite, devour or carry them off; the souls of children it has eaten must fly about in the trees until the grain is brought in. The Roggenwölfe are held to be the children of the Roggenmuhme. Wilhelm Mannhardt compared it with the Norse wolf Fenrir, since both are bound, sometimes with iron.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "roggenwolf-c06",
        "roggenwolf-c07",
        "roggenwolf-c08",
        "roggenwolf-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c01",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s1",
      "quote": "Der Roggenwolf ist ein wolfsgestaltiger Korndämon und Kinderschreck der deutschen Sage, der sich im Feld und Acker aufhält. Verschiedene Sprichwörter des 19. Jahrhunderts charakterisieren den Roggenwolf als unersättlich und gefräßig bis hin zur Bewegungsunfähigkeit als Folge des unmäßigen Schlingens. Auch ist er aggressiv. Der Roggenwolf heult und brüllt in seiner Gier nach Futter.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roggenwolf adalah setan gandum (Korndämon) berwujud serigala dan momok anak-anak dalam legenda Jerman (deutschen), tinggal di ladang; peribahasa abad ke-19 menggambarkannya rakus tak terpuaskan hingga tak bisa bergerak karena kekenyangan, agresif, dan melolong serta meraung karena lapar.",
        "en": "The Roggenwolf is a wolf-shaped corn demon (Korndämon) and children's bogey of German (deutschen) legend dwelling in fields; 19th-century proverbs call it insatiable and gluttonous to the point of immobility, aggressive, howling and roaring in its hunger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c02",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s1",
      "quote": "Der Roggenwolf ist ein Windgeist. Er trägt davon auch den Namen Windwolf. Besonders, wenn der Wind die Getreideähren in wellenförmige Bewegung bringt, hieß es, der Wolf gehe durch oder über das Korn, sei im Korn oder jage die Schafe im Korn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roggenwolf adalah roh angin, sehingga disebut Windwolf; terutama ketika angin membuat bulir gandum bergelombang, orang berkata serigala berjalan melintasi gandum.",
        "en": "The Roggenwolf is a wind spirit, hence called Windwolf; especially when the wind sets the ears waving, it was said the wolf was going through the corn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c03",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s1",
      "quote": "Er soll auch das Mutterkorn verursachen, welches nach ihm Wolf oder Wolfszahn genannt wird. In der letzten Erntegarbe soll der Roggenwolf sitzen. Nach ihm wird auch die letzte Garbe als Wolf bezeichnet. Wer bei der Ernte mit dem Roggenwolf in Kontakt kommt, wird davon krank.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia konon menyebabkan ergot (Mutterkorn), yang karenanya disebut Wolf atau Wolfszahn; ia duduk di berkas panen terakhir, yang juga disebut Wolf, dan siapa pun yang bersentuhan dengannya saat panen jatuh sakit.",
        "en": "It is said to cause ergot (Mutterkorn), therefore called Wolf or Wolfszahn; it sits in the last harvest sheaf, also called Wolf, and whoever touches it at harvest falls ill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c04",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s1",
      "quote": "Dem Treiben des Roggenwolfes im Feld wird entweder die Eigenschaft zugesprochen, dass es die Fruchtbarkeit des Feldes fördere oder gegenteilig, dass es die Fruchtbarkeit des Feldes verringere, zumal der Roggenwolf sich vom Korn des Feldes ernährt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kegiatan Roggenwolf di ladang dianggap menyuburkan ladang, atau sebaliknya mengurangi kesuburannya, apalagi karena ia memakan gandumnya.",
        "en": "The Roggenwolf's activity in the field was thought either to boost its fertility or, conversely, to reduce it, especially as it feeds on the grain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c05",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s1",
      "quote": "Neben Roggenwolf (Roggenwulf) sind dies die Namensformen Getreidewolf, Kornwolf (großer Kornwolf, Kürnwolf, Koorwolf, Koanwolf, Körwolp), Haferwolf (Hawerwolf), Gerstenwolf, Kartoffelwolf (Kantüffelwolf), Graswolf (Grasewolf), Erbswolf und Pflaumenwolf (Plummenwolf).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selain Roggenwolf, nama lainnya mengikuti tanaman ladangnya: Getreidewolf, Kornwolf, Haferwolf, Gerstenwolf, Kartoffelwolf, Graswolf, Erbswolf, dan Pflaumenwolf.",
        "en": "Besides Roggenwolf, its names follow the crop: Getreidewolf, Kornwolf, Haferwolf, Gerstenwolf, Kartoffelwolf, Graswolf, Erbswolf and Pflaumenwolf."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c06",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s1",
      "quote": "Er soll im Feld auf die Kornblumen suchenden oder Ähren pflückenden Kinder warten, um sie zu zerreißen, zu beißen, zu packen, zu fressen oder fortzutragen. Die Seelen der vom Roggenwolf gefressenen Kinder müssen auf den Bäumen umherfliegen, bis das Korn eingefahren wird. Die Roggenwölfe gelten als Kinder der ebenfalls im Kornfeld hausenden Roggenmuhme, eines anderen Kinderschrecks.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roggenwolf menunggu di ladang anak-anak yang mencari bunga jagung (Kornblumen) atau memetik bulir untuk mencabik, menggigit, memakan, atau membawa mereka pergi; jiwa anak yang dimakannya harus melayang di pepohonan sampai panen dibawa masuk; para Roggenwölfe dianggap anak Roggenmuhme, momok lain di ladang.",
        "en": "The Roggenwolf waits in the field for children seeking cornflowers (Kornblumen) or picking ears, to tear, bite, seize, devour or carry them off; the souls of children it ate must fly about the trees until the grain is in; the Roggenwölfe are held to be children of the Roggenmuhme, another field bogey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c07",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s2",
      "quote": "Roggenwolf, německy „žitný vlk“, též Roggenhund (žitný pes), je polní duch v německém folklóru. Číhá v obilí a dáví nepozorné rolníky a děti jsou varovány, že jím budou roztrhány, když půjdou do pole.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roggenwolf (\"serigala gandum hitam\"), juga Roggenhund, adalah roh ladang dalam cerita rakyat Jerman (německém); ia mengintai di gandum dan mencekik petani yang lengah, dan anak-anak diperingatkan akan dicabiknya bila masuk ladang.",
        "en": "The Roggenwolf ('rye wolf'), also Roggenhund, is a field spirit of German (německém) folklore; it lurks in the grain and strangles careless farmers, and children are warned it will tear them apart if they go into the field."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c08",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s1",
      "quote": "Die Funktion des Roggenwolfs als Kinderschreck wird durch die Namen Gelbzahn und Speilzahn verdeutlicht.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fungsi Roggenwolf sebagai momok anak diperjelas oleh nama Gelbzahn (\"gigi kuning\") dan Speilzahn.",
        "en": "The Roggenwolf's role as a children's bogey is shown by the names Gelbzahn ('yellow tooth') and Speilzahn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c09",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s2",
      "quote": "Folklorista Wilhelm Mannhardt srovnal Roggenwolfa se severským Fenrim. Oba vlci jsou spoutáni, někdy i s pomocí železa",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Folklorist Wilhelm Mannhardt membandingkan Roggenwolf dengan Fenrir Nordik (severským Fenrim); kedua serigala itu diikat, kadang dengan besi.",
        "en": "Folklorist Wilhelm Mannhardt compared the Roggenwolf with the Norse Fenrir (severským Fenrim); both wolves are bound, sometimes with iron."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenwolf-c10",
      "source_id": "roggenwolf-s1",
      "quote": "Das Heulen des Windes soll durch den Roggenwolf hervorgerufen werden. Der Roggenwolf tritt auch als Verkörperung der rauen Luft, des Nebels und des Regens auf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lolongan angin konon ditimbulkan Roggenwolf; ia juga tampil sebagai penjelmaan udara kasar, kabut, dan hujan.",
        "en": "The howling of the wind is said to be caused by the Roggenwolf; it also appears as the embodiment of rough air, fog and rain."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

  ]
}
```

```json
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Schimmelreiter (\"penunggang kuda putih\") adalah kuda putih jahat atau penunggang kuda putih dalam cerita rakyat Jermanik di Niedersachsen dan Altmark, lambang bencana laut yang menghancurkan tanggul saat badai.",
    "en": "The Schimmelreiter ('rider on the white horse') is an evil white horse, or a rider on one, in Germanic folklore of Lower Saxony and the Altmark, a symbol of sea disaster that breaks the dykes in storms.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "schimmelreiter-c01",
      "schimmelreiter-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Jermanik, khususnya di Niedersachsen dan Altmark, Schimmelreiter adalah kuda putih jahat atau penunggang di atas kuda putih, lambang bencana laut terburuk: jebolnya tanggul saat badai. Menurut Katharine Briggs, ia hantu yang paling sering disebut dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, dan penduduk desa Niedersachsen berbicara tentang Schimmelreiter yang muncul bila badai mengancam.",
      "en": "In Germanic folklore, especially in Lower Saxony and the Altmark, the Schimmelreiter is an evil white horse or a rider on a white horse, the symbol of the worst sea disaster: dykes breaking in storms. According to Katharine Briggs he is the ghost most often mentioned in German folklore, and villagers of Lower Saxony speak of the Schimmelreiter who appears when a storm threatens.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "schimmelreiter-c01",
        "schimmelreiter-c03",
        "schimmelreiter-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Schimmelreiter tampaknya berasal dari kenangan akan kuda suci bangsa Jermanik; kuda yang dikurbankan dalam ritus chthonic selalu putih, dan sisa kepercayaan itu tampak dalam takhayul tentang Schimmelreiter. Ia dianggap \"utusan dunia bawah\", dan warna putihnya bukan putih langit yang baik, melainkan putih pucat seperti kain kafan yang melambangkan duka dan maut. Pada abad ke-19 ada teori, yang kini dianggap kabur, bahwa Santo Martinus adalah penerus Schimmelreiter dan Odin.",
      "en": "The Schimmelreiter seems to stem from memories of the sacred horse of the Germanic peoples; horses sacrificed in chthonic rites were always white, and traces of that survive in superstitions about the Schimmelreiter. He is seen as an 'emissary of the underworld', and his white is not the good white of the heavens but a pale, shroud-like white standing for mourning and death. In the 19th century there were theories, now judged hazy, that Saint Martin was the successor of the Schimmelreiter and Odin.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "schimmelreiter-c02",
        "schimmelreiter-c04",
        "schimmelreiter-c05",
        "schimmelreiter-c07"
      ]

      "quote": "Le schimmelreiter ou schimmel reiter est, dans le folklore germanique et plus précisément en Basse-Saxe et dans l'Altmark, un cheval blanc maléfique ou un cavalier sur un cheval blanc, symboles de la catastrophe marine, qui détruit les digues pendant les tempêtes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Schimmelreiter, dalam cerita rakyat Jermanik (germanique), khususnya di Niedersachsen (Basse-Saxe; Lower Saxony) dan Altmark, adalah kuda putih jahat atau penunggang kuda putih, lambang bencana laut yang menghancurkan tanggul saat badai.",
        "en": "The Schimmelreiter, in Germanic (germanique) folklore, specifically in Lower Saxony (Basse-Saxe) and the Altmark, is an evil white horse or a rider on one, symbols of sea disaster that destroys dykes during storms."

      "source_id": "schimmelreiter-s1",
      "quote": "Au XIXe siècle, des théories qualifiées de « fumeuses » faisaient de saint Martin le successeur du schimmelreiter et d'Odin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",

      }

    {
      "id": "schimmelreiter-c06",
      "source_id": "schimmelreiter-s2",
      "quote": "De schimmelrijder is in de Germaanse folklore (meer specifiek in Nedersaksen en Altmark) het kwade witte paard, of een ruiter op het witte paard. Het mythisch wezen is het symbool van de ergste maritieme ramp: de vernietiging van dammen in stormen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Schimmelrijder dalam cerita rakyat Jermanik (Germaanse), khususnya di Niedersachsen (Nedersaksen) dan Altmark, adalah kuda putih jahat atau penunggang kuda putih, lambang bencana laut terburuk: hancurnya tanggul dalam badai.",
        "en": "The schimmelrijder in Germanic (Germaanse) folklore, specifically Lower Saxony (Nedersaksen) and Altmark, is the evil white horse or a rider on it, symbol of the worst maritime disaster: dams destroyed in storms."
      }
    },
    {

      "source_id": "schimmelreiter-s1",
      "quote": "Il s'agit d'animaux à la blancheur « nocturne, lunaire, froide et vide », comme un suaire ou un fantôme, ils évoquent le deuil",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuda-kuda ini berwarna putih yang \"malam, bulan, dingin, dan hampa\", seperti kain kafan atau hantu, dan membangkitkan duka.",
```
